# OpenMoHAA native Metal port: plan

Goal (verbatim, see CLAUDE.md): "Create a FULL macOS Silicon Metal native port of openmohaa, with the ultimate goal of stripping OpenGL entirely from the code by the time we're done, and only have it run Metal from start to finish. Look at all possible ways to improve performance while we do it, increase efficiency, etc. Make it as lean and robust as possible."

Branches: `main` = upstream mirror (openmoh/openmohaa a2f34019 at the start). `metal` = this work.

## 1. Findings (session 1 survey)

Paths are relative to `code/` unless they start with `cmake/`.

### 1.1 Build and targets
- CMake ≥ 3.25, Ninja. Options in `CMakeLists.txt:14-40`: `BUILD_RENDERER_GL1` ON, `BUILD_RENDERER_GL2` OFF, `USE_RENDERER_DLOPEN` ON, `USE_OPENAL(_DLOPEN)` ON, `BUILD_SERVER` ON, `BUILD_MACOS_APP` ON (`cmake/platforms/macos.cmake:7`).
- Targets: client `openmohaa` (.app bundle), server `omohaaded`, `renderer_opengl1.dylib` (moved into `openmohaa.app/Contents/MacOS` by `finish_macos_app`), `cgame.dylib`, `game.dylib`, plus launchers (broken on macOS: they read `/proc/self/exe`).
- Install layout: everything goes to `<prefix>/lib/openmohaa/`. `fs_basepath` defaults to the folder that holds the .app. Libraries are found in `Contents/MacOS`, then in `fs_basepath` (`sys/sys_main.c:511`).
- `cmake/platforms/macos.cmake:17-18` set **deployment target 11.0 and universal arm64;x86_64** as plain variables, which override `-D` flags. Phase 1 changed them to **26.0 and arm64** (session 2). Every renderer gets `-framework OpenGL` (`:15`); `cmake/renderer_metal.cmake` removes it again for the Metal renderer.
- `find_package(OpenAL)` picks Apple's OpenAL.framework, which has no `alext.h`, and the build fails. `port/build.sh` passes `-DCMAKE_DISABLE_FIND_PACKAGE_OpenAL=ON` (internal openal-soft headers; the library is dlopen'ed as `libopenal.1.dylib`, `client/snd_openal_new.cpp:124`). Nothing builds openal-soft locally, so `port/run.sh` links Homebrew's next to the exe.
- `cmake/libraries/jpeg.cmake:1` and `freetype.cmake:5` only worked when a GL renderer is built (widened for `BUILD_RENDERER_METAL` in session 2). There is also a `JPEG_DEFINTIONS` typo at `jpeg.cmake:22`; left as is, because fixing it adds `USE_INTERNAL_JPEG`/`JPEG_INTERNALS` to GL1's compile.
- Flex/Bison output is written into the source tree (`code/parser/generated`, gitignored). CTest finds no tests.
- Internal SDL is a prebuilt universal `thirdparty/libs/macos/libSDL2-2.0.0.dylib` (2.32.8). zlib, jpeg-9f, ogg, vorbis, opus, libmad are compiled from `thirdparty/`.

### 1.2 Renderer interface
- The real header is `renderercommon/tr_public.h`. `renderergl1/tr_public.h` is dead (`#if 0`). `REF_API_VERSION` is 14.
- `refexport_t` has 85 entries. Groups: frame/scene, 2D (`DrawStretchPic`, `DrawTilePic`, `DrawStretchRaw`, `Set2DWindow`, `Scissor`, `DrawBox`, `DrawLineLoop`, …), TIKI/model (`RegisterModel`, `LerpTag`, `ForceUpdatePose`, `TIKI_Orientation`, swipes, …), fonts (`LoadFont` .RitualFont, `DrawString`), images/shaders, MOHAA world queries (`LightForPoint`, `GetLightingForDecal`, `MarkFragments`, `inPVS`, …). `GetRefAPI` is at `renderergl1/tr_init.c:1847`.
- **Upstream bug:** `SwipePoint` and `DrawBackground` are never assigned and stay NULL, though cgame calls `re.SwipePoint` (`cgame/cg_swipe.cpp:221`). The renderer implements `RE_SwipePoint` (`tr_swipe.cpp:88`).
- `refimport_t` has 87 functions + 4 data pointers, including TIKI/skeletor imports (`TIKI_SetPoseInternal`, `GetFrameInternal`, `SKEL_GetMorphWeightFrame`, …).
- Loading: `CL_InitRef` (`client/cl_main.cpp:3190`). The `cl_renderer` cvar (default `opengl1`, ARCHIVE|LATCH) selects `renderer_<name>.dylib`, falling back to the default. Symbol `GetRefAPI`.
- `glconfig_t` (`renderercommon/tr_types.h:212`) is copied field by field into cgame (`client/cl_cgame.cpp:49-73`), so **its layout is ABI and must not change**. Fields actually read: `vidWidth/vidHeight` (HUD, UI, input), `isFullscreen`, `stereoEnabled`, `renderer_string`, `hardwareType`/`maxTextureSize` (cinematics).

### 1.3 renderergl1 (what gets ported; ~43k lines)
- **The renderer owns the window**: `sdl/sdl_glimp.c` and `sdl/sdl_gamma.c` are compiled into the renderer. `GLimp_SetMode` creates the SDL window + GL context, and `GLimp_Init` calls `ri.IN_Init(SDL_window)` (`sdl_glimp.c:1179`).
- qgl is loaded at runtime (`SDL_GL_GetProcAddress`, `sdl_glimp.c:262`) through X-macros in `renderercommon/qgl.h`. About 73 distinct GL entry points are used. qgl lines per file: tr_backend 142, tr_shade 124, tr_draw 66, tr_shadows 59, tr_sphere_shade 44 (debug lines), tr_init 39, tr_main 28, tr_image 28, tr_model 25, tr_surface 19, tr_sky 17, tr_cmds 14, tr_flares 11, tr_sun_flare 9, sdl_glimp 60. Terrain, BSP, world, font, util, static models and marks have none.
- Single-threaded command queue (no SMP). `GL_State` (`tr_backend.c:220`) maps `GLS_*` bits (blend, depth, alpha test incl. foliage thresholds, fog) to GL state. Other key functions: `GL_Bind`, `GL_SelectTexture`, `GL_TexEnv`.
- **2D is immediate, not queued.** `Draw_*`/`Set2DWindow` (`tr_draw.c`) call `R_IssuePendingRenderCommands()` and then draw at once (Set2DWindow calls `glViewport`/`glOrtho` directly). The UI calls Set2DWindow around the 3D view. A Metal backend must be able to encode at any point in the frame.
- `tr_shade.c`: client-side vertex arrays from `tess` (positions as padded vec4). Per stage, the CPU fills `tess.svars` (`ComputeTexCoords`/`ComputeColors`, deforms/tcMods in `tr_shade_calc.c`), then issues one draw. Two TMUs max (`DrawMultitextured` + `GL_TexEnv`). `MAX_SHADER_STAGES` 8, `SHADER_MAX_VERTEXES` 2048.
- **`R_DrawElements` (`tr_shade.c:163`): `r_primitives 0` picks glDrawElements only if `GL_EXT_compiled_vertex_array` exists. Apple's GL doesn't have it, so the default path is `R_DrawStripElements` with `glArrayElement` inside `glBegin`/`glEnd` (immediate mode). `r_primitives 2` forces glDrawElements, and the user plays with it.**
- `tr_shader.c`: MOHAA keywords (`nextBundle`, `animMapOnce/Phase`, `ifCvar`, `#if` blocks for `r_textureDetails`, `portalsky`, `spritegen`, `force32bit`, `clampTime`, `lightglow` deform; rgbGen `lightingSpherical`; alphaGen `tikiDistFade`, `heightFade`, `dotView`, `skyAlpha`; tcGen `environmentmodel`, `sunreflection`; tcMod `wavetrans`, `parallax`, `macro`; `nofog`).
- `tr_image.c`: its own TGA/JPG/BMP/PCX/DDS/GST loaders. `Upload32` resamples to power of two, applies picmip, generates mipmaps on the CPU (`R_MipMap`) and always uploads RGBA8. DDS uploads as S3TC (off by default). Gamma/overbright: `R_SetColorMappings` (`:2758`) enables overbright only with hardware gamma **and** fullscreen. Otherwise `R_LightScaleTexture` bakes gamma into the texels.
- Sky: Q3 skybox in immediate mode (`tr_sky.c`) and the MOHAA portal sky (`tr_sky_portal.cpp`, re-runs `R_RenderView`). Flares: Q3 flares read depth with `glReadPixels` (off by default); MOHAA sun/lens flares draw orthographic quads (`tr_sun_flare.cpp`).
- Fog: fixed-function **linear distance fog only** (farplane), with `GLS_FOG*` bits for view/black/white fog color. No Q3 fog volumes. Mirrors/portals: `glClipPlane`.
- Dlights: baked on the CPU into copies of the lightmaps and re-uploaded with `glTexSubImage2D` (`tr_light.c`, `R_UploadDlights`). Entities get dlights through sphere lighting (`tr_sphere_shade.cpp`, CPU per-vertex light-grid + light lighting).
- Also: `tr_terrain.c` (ROAM-style LOD terrain with CPU split/merge and geomorph), `tr_staticmodels.cpp` (static TIKIs skinned once at load), `tr_ghost.cpp` (CPU-animated textures re-uploaded each frame), `tr_marks_permanent.c` (`.dcl` decals), `tr_shadows.c` (stencil shadow volumes, immediate mode), `tr_font.cpp` (RitualFont through tess), `tr_util.cpp` (immediate-mode emulation into tess). Screenshots read back with `glReadPixels(GL_RGB)` (`tr_init.c:498`). Cinematics go through `RE_StretchRaw` (`tr_backend.c:1008`), which calls `qglFinish` and uploads each frame.
- `renderergl2` is maintained upstream but off by default. It also skins on the CPU. Not used by this port.

### 1.4 GL outside the renderer
- None in client/cgame/fgame/uilib. They draw only through `re.*`/`cgi.*`/`uii.*`. The leaks are `renderercommon/qgl.h` (includes `SDL_opengl.h`), `tr_common.h` (`#include "qgl.h"`, `image_t.texnum` is a `GLuint`, GLimp declarations), `new/tr_types_new.h` (`OPENGL_DRIVER_NAME`), and `sdl_glimp.c`/`sdl_gamma.c`. `tools/` and the gamespy samples have GL but aren't built.
- `cl_avi.cpp` video capture is effectively disabled. `CL_RefCIN_UploadCinematic` is an empty stub, so in-world videoMaps are unsupported upstream.

### 1.5 TIKI / skeletor
- `skeletor/*` and `tiki/*` are compiled into the client and server only. The renderer compiles just `tiki/tiki_mesh.cpp` (bone/morph caches) and reaches the rest through `ri.TIKI_*`/`ri.SKEL_*`.
- Skinning is all on the CPU: `R_AddSkelSurfaces` (`tr_model.cpp:720`) → `ri.GetFrameInternal` → `skeletor_c::GetFrame` → bone matrices in `skelBoneCache_t` → `RB_SkelMesh` (`tr_model.cpp:1022`) skins into `tess.xyz/normal` in the backend. Vertices are variable-length (`skeletorVertex_t` + morphs + weights, no fixed cap on weights). Normals use the first bone only. `TIKI_MAX_BONES` 100, `TIKI_MAX_VERTEXES` 1000. LOD uses progressive-mesh collapse maps.

### 1.6 SDL, input, audio
- SDL2 only (2.32.8). Video and window in the renderer. Input in the client (`sdl/sdl_input.c`: keyboard, text input, relative mouse + grab, joystick/gamecontroller, window events, DROPFILE for the `openmohaa://` URL). Colour cursor in `sdl_mouse.c`. Clipboard. `sdl_snd.c` is not compiled (audio doesn't use SDL). No HiDPI support (`SDL_WINDOW_ALLOW_HIGHDPI` unused, no `NSHighResolutionCapable`).
- Audio: the active system is the MOHAA `*_new.cpp` one on OpenAL only (`NO_MODERN_DMA` undefined); there is no fallback. 3D positional sources, 16-buffer streaming for music/dialog/movies (polled, no thread), ALC_SOFT HRTF/limiter/resampler extensions. EFX reverb is a FIXME no-op. About 7.1k lines of ioq3 sound code are present but not compiled.

### 1.7 Other platforms
- Windows only: `sys/sys_win32.c`, `con_win32.c`, `sys/new/sys_win32_new.c`, .rc/manifest. `win_bounds.cpp` and `win_localization.cpp` are built everywhere despite their names. `asm/` is x86 only and not built on clang. `null/` is used by the dedicated server. `Launcher/` doesn't work on macOS. `sys_osx.m` has only `Sys_Dialog` (NSAlert) and `Sys_StripAppBundle`; it imports Carbon without using it, and IOKit is linked but unused. gamespy is 178k lines (only common/gcdkey/natneg/qr2 are built). Platform `#ifdef` density is low. **Phase 7a (session 15) deleted all of these except `null/`** (Phase 7, "Session 15 result"); the platform `#ifdef`s inside shared files stay.

### 1.8 Ideas from the user's Vulkan port (pstngh/openmohaa-vulkan, branch `vulkan-renderer`; ideas only, no code copied)
- `code/renderervk` is a copy of renderergl1 with every GL call replaced by a GL-free backend API that mirrors GL1's fixed-function state (`GLS_*` bits, texenv, cull, depth range, polygon offset, fog, clip plane, stencil, matrices, viewport/scissor, draw/clear/readback, texture create/upload/destroy). With that, the `tr_*` code barely changes and upstream renderer fixes keep porting. They added an immediate-mode emulator (`tr_immediate.c`), GPU static geometry and GPU skinning.
- Outside the renderer they changed only CMake (new renderer target, jpeg/freetype guards, macos.cmake bundle placement), `tr_common.h` (skip qgl.h, `texnum` → `unsigned int`), the `cl_renderer` default, and a new `sdl_vkimp.c` window file. The refexport/refimport ABI is unchanged.
- Gotchas they recorded:
  - Hidden visibility (only `GetRefAPI` exported) was their biggest single CPU win.
  - Keep GL projection matrices and remap clip z; mind the bottom-left window origin for viewport/scissor/readback; keep CCW front faces.
  - Reproduce the exact alpha-test thresholds, "no destination alpha" (RGB textures sample alpha = 1), Mesa-style fog, clamping stencil ops.
  - Never reuse texture handles while frames are in flight. Uploads mid-frame (cinematics, ghosts) must not change already recorded draws. Free GPU objects only after the frame's fence.
  - Frames start lazily (2D exports flush render commands). Take screenshots at end of frame (GL1 read a stale back buffer).
  - Capability flags come from the same `r_ext_*` cvars so shader collapsing matches. A default stencil of 0 bits disables `r_shadows 2`.
  - Deterministic screenshot testing: `devmap`, `fixedtime 16`, `thereisnomonkey 1`, `tele`/`face`, `pause` before `screenshot`; delete `main/configs` between runs; GL1 sometimes saves an all-black frame.

---

## 2. Baseline (upstream GL1, a2f34019 + the timedemo work metric)

Machine: Mac mini M4 (4P+6E, 10-core GPU, 16 GB), macOS 26.6.2, Xcode 27.1. Display: DELL G2524H 1920×1080 @ 240 Hz (main) + DELL U4323QE 4K at 2× (second).

**Build** (`port/build.sh gl`): Release, ThinLTO, universal arm64+x86_64 (forced by macos.cmake), deployment target 11.0. 85 s on 10 cores. GL context reports "2.1 Metal - 90.5": Apple's GL runs on Metal internally.

**arm64-only rebuild** (session 2, after Phase 1 set arm64 + macOS 26.0): build 36 s. Same bench, same binary, two sets: 937.1 / 941.1 / 941.1 fps = **0.896 / 0.895 / 0.893 ms work**, and later 909.4 / 925.5 / 912.5 fps = 0.954 / 0.922 / 0.941 ms. GL1 from the combined `metal` install (`cl_renderer opengl1`): 0.901 / 0.943 / 0.940 ms. So the arm64-only GL1 reference is **~0.90–0.95 ms work/frame**; between sets the noise is about ±3% (within a set ±0.3%), larger than session 1 measured. Compare against GL1 runs made in the same sitting.

**Benchmark** (`port/bench.sh run gl 3`): timedemo of `port/bench/bench_mohdm6.dm_8` (dm/mohdm6 Stalingrad, FFA, 8 bots, spectator following bots, 120 s, 2398 frames; sha256 `c62f7972735b60c15b32392168f41235849407b637c9ac6004d68a36312320a2`), 1280×720 window on the main display, `r_swapInterval 0`, `com_maxfps 0`, sound muted, fresh default config each run.

| Config | fps (3 runs) | wall ms/frame | **work ms/frame** | GPU ms/frame (`trace`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GL1 `r_primitives 2` (**reference**: the user's setting, GL's fastest path) | 923.4 / 925.9 / 926.2 | 1.081 | **0.934** (limiter wait 0.146) | **0.18** (16% GPU utilization in the timedemo) |
| GL1 `r_primitives 0` (upstream default) | 293.2 / 296.3 / 295.9 | 3.39 | 3.39 | ~0.6 (older estimate method) |

- **"work" is new.** The engine never runs a frame shorter than 1 ms: since Quake 3, `Com_Frame` pads frames to its 1 ms minimum with an integer-ms clock. So fps saturates near 1000 (with `com_maxfps 0` or in a timedemo; `com_maxfps` above 1000 removes the floor but makes the game clock run fast, see §4b rule 7), and GL with `r_primitives 2` already sits there. The timedemo now also prints `timedemo work: X ms/frame` (wall time minus Com_Frame's frame-limiter wait, µs clock; `qcommon/common.c` `com_timedemoWaitUsec`, printed in `client/cl_main.cpp CL_DemoCompleted`). **Compare renderers on work ms/frame.** It only measures; it changes nothing in how frames run.
- Run-to-run noise: ±0.3% for `r_primitives 2` and ~3% for `r_primitives 0` (one set gave 286–294 fps). Under `sample` the run is ~4% slower, under xctrace ~5–10% slower.
- **Where the GL time goes** (`sample` profiles; `port/sample_by_library.py`):
  - `r_primitives 2`: the frame is dominated by `RB_SwapBuffers`. It calls `qglFinish()` every frame (`tr_backend.c:1429`), and the CPU blocks in `GLDShareGroupRec::waitUsage` until the GPU is done, then `CGLFlushDrawable` presents. The GPU is busy only 0.18 ms of each ~1 ms frame, so most of the frame is CPU↔GPU **synchronization latency**, not rendering. The engine's own CPU work is small.
  - `r_primitives 0`: ~1.96 ms/frame in `RB_SwapBuffers` (≈half glFinish wait, half present) and ~1.7 ms/frame drawing the 3D view/HUD (`UIWindowManager::UpdateViews`). Of the latter, ~65% is Apple's GL driver (AGX, AppleMetalOpenGLRenderer, GLEngine, malloc churn from immediate mode) and ~13% renderer code (top: `RB_Light_Real` 4.7%, `RB_SkelMesh` 2.4%, `R_DrawStripElements` 1.3%).
  - Apple's GL splits every game frame into ~8–10 GPU submissions (render passes): 23806 trace frames for ~2400 game frames. On a tile-based GPU each extra pass costs tile loads/stores.
- **Metal, Phase 1** (session 2; draws are still stubs, so this is the floor of front end + backend frame overhead, not comparable with GL1): `port/bench.sh run metal 1 +set cl_renderer metal`: 998.8 fps, **0.318 ms work/frame**, no wait for drawables or GPU. With `r_swapInterval 1`: 239.1 fps, the ~3.5 ms vsync wait sits at the end of the frame (before the next input sample).
- **Metal, Phase 2a** (session 3, 2026-10-17; the whole renderer now draws, see Phase 2's result). Same sitting, `port/bench.sh run gl 3` and `port/bench.sh run metal 3 +set cl_renderer metal`, **with the screen locked** (`CGSSessionScreenIsLocked`: nothing is composited, so GL1's swap is cheaper than in §2's table and Metal gets a drawable for only ~1 frame in 15; re-measure both unlocked):

  | Config | fps (3 runs) | **work ms/frame** | GPU ms/frame |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | GL1 `r_primitives 2` | 975.2 / 982.0 / 981.2 | **0.766 / 0.759 / 0.765** | (no trace) |
  | Metal, 2 frames in flight (default) | 1000.0 / 1000.0 / 1000.0 | **0.359 / 0.364 / 0.361** | 0.11 (frame command buffer GPU time, `r_metalPresentStats 1`) |
  | Metal, `r_metalFramesInFlight 1` | 998.3 | 0.707 (0.31 ms/frame waiting for the GPU, after the commit) | |
  | Metal, `r_metalFramesInFlight 3` | 1000.0 | 0.361 | |

  - **2.1× less work per frame than GL1, under the Phase 2 target (≤ 0.45 ms).** fps is pinned at the engine's 1 ms floor.
  - CPU profile (`sample`, 8 s of real-time demo playback): 74% `select` + 7.6% `mach_absolute_time` = the engine's frame limiter polling. Of the busy time: renderer front end ≈ 9% of all samples (`RB_SkelMesh` 2.7%, `RB_Light_Real` 1.9%, `RB_SurfaceFace` 0.8%), Metal driver (AGX + IOGPU) ≈ 2%, ring copies (`memmove`) 0.9%, the backend's own functions ≈ 0.2%. GL1 spent ~65% of its busy time in Apple's GL driver.
  - Two measured traps, both fixed (see §3.2 "Added in session 3"): with the present in the frame's command buffer, completion came only at the display's next refresh while the screen was locked (runs 1 and 3 ran at 239 fps, 3.2 ms/frame waiting for a frame slot); and completed handlers arrived ~1.3 ms after the GPU had finished (0.97 ms work). Frame completion now comes from an `MTLSharedEvent`.
- **Metal, Phase 2b** (session 4, 2026-10-17, **screen unlocked**: `CGSSessionScreenIsLocked` absent before and after). Same sitting, `port/bench.sh run gl 3` and `port/bench.sh run metal 3 +set cl_renderer metal +set r_metalPresentStats 1`, then one run each with `+set r_swapInterval 1`:

  | Config | fps | **work ms/frame** | notes |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | GL1 `r_primitives 2` | 902.2 / 938.2 / 943.4 | **0.955 / 0.887 / 0.884** | |
  | Metal (2 frames in flight, 2 drawables) | 999.2 / 997.9 / 997.5 | **0.393 / 0.396 / 0.389** | GPU 0.12 ms/frame; 58 draws, 1 pass, 143 KB ring per frame; 12 pipelines; ~40% of frames get a drawable (the compositor shows ~240/s), the rest skip their present without waiting; every presented frame was shown |
  | GL1, `r_swapInterval 1` | 239.5 | (4.173: includes the vsync wait) | |
  | Metal, `r_swapInterval 1` | 237.0 | (4.218: includes the vsync wait) | 3.47 ms/frame waiting in `nextDrawable`, at the end of the frame (§4b rule 1); every frame presented and shown |

  - **2.3× less work per frame than GL1 with the compositor running**, under the Phase 2 target (≤ 0.45 ms). Metal's 0.39 ms vs 0.36 ms on a locked screen (session 3) is the cost of really getting and presenting drawables.
  - 4K@2× (U4323QE, `+set r_displayIndex 1`, 1280×720-point window): Metal draws 2560×1440 pixels, GPU 1.2 ms/frame (not benchmarked; the display runs at 60 Hz). GL1 renders the same window at 1280×720 and lets macOS upscale it.
- **Metal, Phase 2c** (session 5, 2026-10-17, screen unlocked). Bench in the same sitting (`port/bench.sh run gl 3`, `port/bench.sh run metal 3 +set cl_renderer metal`): GL1 **1.082 / 1.087 / 1.091 ms** work (GL1 ran ~15% slower than in session 4 this sitting), Metal **0.412 / 0.403 / 0.397** and 0.397 / 0.398 ms. With the probe on (`+set cl_latencyStats 1`; a timedemo has no mouse input, but every frame is still tagged and recorded with both handlers): 0.409 / 0.400 / 0.401 / 0.400 ms (one run 0.462). **The probe costs nothing measurable.**
- **Input → present latency** (session 5, `port/latency.sh`): dm/mohdm6 in single-player mode on a local server; 20 s of synthetic 1000 Hz mouse movement posted at the HID event tap (§3.2 "Added in session 5"); `com_maxfps 250` (the user's cap) unless noted; vsync off unless noted; 2 frames in flight, 2 drawables; the 240 Hz G2524H. Values in ms, p50 / p95 / p99.
  - *pump* = when the frame's SDL event pump started (its input sample).
  - *event* = the OS timestamp of the newest mouse event in that pump.
  - *present* = the display swap that first shows the input (checked against Instruments, below).

  | Mode | Frames with input | event → pump | pump → commit | pump → GPU done | **pump → present** | **event → present** |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Metal, 1280×720 window, 250 fps | 4979 (96% shown by their own frame) | 0.98 / 1.92 / 2.05 | 0.31 / 0.46 / 0.55 | 0.75 / 1.24 / 1.38 | **10.02 / 12.16 / 12.35** | **10.90 / 13.19 / 13.40** |
  | Metal, window, ~1000 fps (`com_maxfps 0`) | 13965 (32% own; 68% by a later frame, no drawable ready) | 0.57 / 1.51 / 1.67 | 0.29 / 0.43 / 0.50 | 0.62 / 0.94 / 1.16 | **5.07 / 8.91 / 10.98** | **5.72 / 9.60 / 11.65** |
  | Metal, window, 250 fps, vsync on | 4741 (100% own) | 0.80 / 1.22 / 1.70 | 0.34 / 0.44 / 0.50 | 1.21 / 1.35 / 1.39 | **7.72 / 11.88 / 11.89** | **8.72 / 13.06 / 13.10** |
  | Metal, 1920×1080 fullscreen, 250 fps | 4992 | 0.76 / 0.88 / 0.92 | 0.25 / 0.31 / 0.35 | 1.02 / 1.49 / 1.70 | not measurable (see below) | |
  | Metal, fullscreen, ~1000 fps | 15846 (33% own) | 0.57 / 1.54 / 1.72 | 0.28 / 0.38 / 0.41 | 0.64 / 1.28 / 1.51 | 5.05 / 9.78 / 12.19 (upper bound, see below) | 5.93 / 10.38 / 12.73 |
  | Metal, fullscreen, 250 fps, vsync on | 4739 (100% own) | 0.79 / 1.34 / 1.68 | 0.28 / 0.37 / 0.44 | 2.12 / 2.51 / 2.60 | **11.93 / 11.94 / 11.94** | **12.66 / 13.24 / 13.52** |
  | GL1, window, 250 fps | 4988 | 1.07 / 1.37 / 2.07 | | | pump → EndFrame return (after glFinish + SwapBuffers; no present time, a lower bound): 0.68 / 1.35 / 1.60 | event → EndFrame return: 1.68 / 2.49 / 2.85 |
  | GL1, fullscreen, 250 fps | 4945 | 0.50 / 1.51 / 1.67 | | | 0.56 / 0.87 / 1.29 (same lower bound) | 1.09 / 2.10 / 2.41 |

  - **The probe's present times are real.** `TRACE=1 LATSTATS=2 port/latency.sh …` records Instruments' Metal System Trace during the run, and `port/display_trace.py` matches every present time the probe reported against the display's own swap log. Window: 1557 of 1561 present times fall within ±1 µs on a display swap that shows that very frame. Fullscreen at ~1000 fps: 2392 of 2396.
  - **A composited window costs about two refreshes, in two modes.** At 250 fps, commit → present sits at either ~5 ms or ~9.7 ms (one 240 Hz refresh apart) for stretches of 0.5–5 s, then switches (the probe's time series; Instruments agrees). At ~1000 fps the median falls to 5.1 ms: only frames that find a ready drawable are presented, and they arrive just after the compositor released the previous drawable. Phase 5: find what selects the mode (present timing, drawable count, `CAMetalDisplayLink`).
  - **Fullscreen is not direct-to-display, in either renderer.** Instruments: Metal's fullscreen layer "cannot go direct to display due to layer requires a potential offscreen render"; GL1's fullscreen surface isn't direct either. So fullscreen goes through the compositor like a window (Phase 3).
  - **Fullscreen with vsync off: Core Animation's present feedback is wrong.** Share of presented drawables reported as never shown (presented-handler status 3; `presentedTime` 0):
    - 98–99% at 250 fps;
    - ~33% at ~1000 fps;
    - almost all at 200 and 125 fps.

    Yet Instruments shows the display swapping game frames 220–240 times a second. Where Core Animation does give a time, it's right. So the probe leaves its present lines out when more than half of the presented frames are reported as never shown (fullscreen 250 fps above). In fullscreen at ~1000 fps, pump → present is an upper bound: the input of a frame reported as never shown is credited to the next frame that has a time. Re-check once fullscreen goes direct.
  - **Vsync on queues a frame.** Fullscreen at 250 fps with vsync: 11.9 ms, constant (2 drawables: each frame waits a refresh behind the one on screen). Window with vsync: 7.7 ms p50. With vsync on, the GPU also finishes 1.8 ms after the commit, against ~0.45 ms without vsync (not explained yet). Phase 5's low-latency vsync (1 frame in flight, `CAMetalDisplayLink`, just-in-time frame start) targets both.
  - **Commit → GPU done takes 0.35–0.45 ms** for ~0.12 ms of GPU work: the GPU starts ~0.3 ms after the commit (session 3 measured the same). So Metal's GPU done (0.75 ms p50 in a window) lands where GL1's EndFrame returns (0.68 ms, after its glFinish), although Metal commits at 0.31 ms. The submission gap is a Phase 5 target.
  - **event → pump** is the OS-side wait plus SDL's pump: ~0–2 ms for 1000 Hz events at 250 fps. The oldest and newest event of a pump are almost always the same: macOS coalesces mouse moves into one event per pump.
  - Caveat: on the local server, `SV_Frame` (the 20 Hz game frame) runs between the input pump and the usercmd on about 1 frame in 12 at 250 fps. Online clients don't do that work, so it can only inflate pump → commit's p95/p99.
- **Metal, Phase 3a** (session 6, 2026-10-17, screen unlocked). `port/bench.sh run gl 3` and `port/bench.sh run metal 3 +set cl_renderer metal`, same sitting: GL1 **0.928 / 0.937 / 0.917 ms** work, Metal **0.395 / 0.397 / 0.391 ms**. The bench runs in a window, so the display-mode changes don't touch it.
- **Metal, Phase 3b** (session 7, 2026-10-17, screen unlocked, the user active on the machine). Same commands, same sitting, with the compiler-bug flag (§3.2 session 7): GL1 **0.931 / 0.958 / 1.048 ms** work, Metal **0.409 / 0.443 / 0.408 ms**. Before the flag, in an earlier sitting: GL1 1.086 / 0.983 / 0.959, Metal 0.485 / 0.410 / 0.443. No renderer code changed since 3a. Both renderers vary together with the load, so the ratio (~2.2×) is the comparable figure.
- **Metal, Phase 4** (session 9, 2026-10-17, screen unlocked; OpenGL deleted, the renderer linked into the client). GL1 now comes from the `gl` reference flavor (an export of c9dddadd, the last commit with GL1; `port/build.sh gl`). Same sitting, `port/bench.sh run gl 3` and `port/bench.sh run metal 3`: GL1 **0.921 / 0.923 / 0.909 ms** work, Metal **0.413 / 0.416 / 0.408 ms**. Static vs dylib, A/B in one earlier sitting (the pre-deletion GL1+Metal build from the same export, `cl_renderer metal`): dylib 0.424 / 0.423 / 0.423 and 0.422 / 0.418 / 0.413, static 0.430 / 0.425 / 0.411: **the static link changes nothing measurable.** This sitting ran ~0.015 ms slower than session 8 (0.40) for both builds.

  - *fullscreen*: exclusive, `r_fullscreen 1`, 1920×1080;
  - *borderless*: `r_fullscreen 1` + `r_noborder 1`, a native fullscreen Space at the desktop's 1920×1080.

  Drawables: 2, except fullscreen/borderless with vsync, where the new automatic choice uses 3. Values in ms, p50 / p95 / p99.

  | window, ~1000 fps | 16359 (20% own) | 0.26 / 0.35 / 0.43 | 0.58 / 0.75 / 0.85 | **12.91 / 14.78 / 15.10** (traced run: 4.91 / 8.36 / 10.06) | 13.64 / 15.34 / 15.48 |
  | window, 250 fps, vsync | 4793 (100%) | 0.34 / 0.52 / 0.75 | 1.02 / 1.29 / 1.37 | **11.86 / 11.89 / 11.90** | 12.77 / 13.41 / 13.90 |
  | window, ~1000 fps, vsync | 4793 (100%) | 0.34 / 0.47 / 0.56 | 1.18 / 1.38 / 1.46 | **11.86 / 11.89 / 11.90** | 12.78 / 13.34 / 13.84 |

  - **Both fullscreen modes go direct to display, on every frame** (Instruments: "direct to display: Yes" for every swap with a game frame). With vsync off the display flips as soon as a frame arrives (tearing): 250 flips/s at 250 fps, ~480/s at ~1000 fps. **pump → present is 2.1 ms at 250 fps** (p99 2.4), against 10.0 ms in session 5's window, and fullscreen couldn't be measured at all before. Exclusive and borderless are the same.
  - **Core Animation's present feedback is right once the layer is direct.** Every presented handler reports a time (status 2), and the probe's present times land exactly on the display's swaps:
    - fullscreen 250 fps: 1661 of 1667 within 2 µs, 1657 showing that very frame;
    - borderless 250 fps: 1632 of 1635;
    - fullscreen ~1000 fps: 3082 of 3087;
    - fullscreen with vsync: 829 of 831.

    Session 5's "never shown" reports came from the composited fullscreen. The probe's fullscreen numbers are now real, at every rate.
  - **Where the 2.1 ms goes (250 fps, vsync off):** commit at 0.22 ms; the GPU done 0.74 ms later, for ~0.12 ms of GPU work (session 5's submission gap); the flip ~1.1 ms after that. Both gaps are Phase 5 targets.
  - **~1000 fps, vsync off:** only ~half the frames find a free drawable (the drawable that was flipped away comes back ~2 ms later). A frame without one skips its present, and its input waits for the next presented frame: p50 2.2–2.35 ms.
    - **3 drawables don't help:** more frames are presented (24348 vs 19520 in 41 s), but 26% of them are never shown, and latency goes up (3.42 / 5.74 / 6.95).
    - At 250 fps, 3 drawables also cost a little: 2.41 / 2.81 / 4.66.

    So 2 stay the default without vsync.
  - **Vsync direct to display needed a third drawable.** With 2, fullscreen and borderless ran at **125 fps** instead of 240:
    - every frame waited 7.25 ms for a drawable;
    - Instruments: 125 flips/s;
    - pump → present was already 8.05 ms.

    That would also have halved the command rate (§4b rule 7). With 3 drawables they run at 240 fps with the same 8.06 ms, constant; `r_metalDrawables` now defaults to 0 = automatic (§3.2 "Added in session 6"). This is two refreshes minus the frame's own work: the vsync queue is the Phase 5 low-latency-vsync target (part 4). Session 5's composited fullscreen with vsync took 11.9 ms.
  - **The window is never direct** ("display rejected"), and today's compositor behaved differently from session 5's: it swapped the display at the game's present rate (249 swaps/s at 250 fps, 436/s at ~1000 fps) instead of 240 Hz. So the window's numbers swing between runs: at 250 fps p50 2.86 and 2.38 ms (p95 ~10–12); at ~1000 fps 12.91 and 4.91 ms. Session 5 measured 10.0 and 5.1. With vsync the window holds 11.86 ms (session 5: 7.7). **Fullscreen is the low-latency mode:** stable ~2 ms vs anything from 2.4 to 13 ms windowed.
  - **event → pump depends on each run's phase** between the synthetic 1000 Hz events and the 4 ms frame (p50 0.44–1.28 ms, sometimes with p50 ≈ p95), not on the display mode. Compare modes on pump → present.
  - **Game Mode turns on in both fullscreen modes** (gamepolicyd log), not in the window. It wasn't measured on vs off (Phase 5).
- **Input → present latency, SDL against the native layer** (session 23, 2026-10-18, Phase 6 slice 6f's synthetic half, §3.1 #17; the screen unlocked before every run, load 3–5, 5–9 during the traced runs). `port/latency.sh` as in session 6 (dm/mohdm6, 20 s of synthetic 1000 Hz mouse, `com_maxfps 250`, vsync off, 2 frames in flight, 2 drawables, the 240 Hz G2524H), `OMM_FLAVOR=metal` (SDL, the 6f baseline: the same code since 6b) against `OMM_FLAVOR=native`, both at `in_mouseRaw 0` (the movement events; GCMouse never sees the synthetic mouse), alternating per run (SDL, native, native, SDL, SDL, native), 3 runs per flavor and mode plus one traced run each. The runs' whole-run percentiles, median of the 3 runs, ms, p50 / p95 / p99:

  | exclusive 1920×1080 (`+set r_fullscreen 1 +set r_customwidth 1920 +set r_customheight 1080`) | 4936–4994 | SDL 8.9%, native 11.4% | **2.23 / 6.01 / 6.36** (2.12–2.26) | **2.21 / 6.03 / 6.36** (2.21–2.26) | 2.38 / 6.24 / 6.64; 2.32 / 6.14 / 6.51 |
  | borderless, the Space (`+set r_fullscreen 1 +set r_noborder 1`) | 4940–4994 | 0.1% | **2.16 / 2.41 / 2.56** (2.16–2.17) | **2.14 / 2.38 / 2.47** (2.08–2.14) | 2.16 / 2.47 / 2.66; 2.24 / 2.49 / 2.62 |
  | borderless window (the same + `r_borderlessWindow 1`, native only) | 4940–4990 | 8.7% | | **2.17 / 5.95 / 6.22** (2.14–2.23) | –; 2.32 / 6.11 / 6.53 |

  - **The native layer matches SDL in every mode**: pump → present within ±0.1 ms at p50, p95 and p99, inside the runs' spread. One native window run had a p99 of 11.15: its last two 1000-frame windows were in the compositor's slow mode (p95 8.6 and 10.4; sessions 5 and 6), the other runs' windows weren't. pump → commit (0.23–0.40 ms p50) and pump → GPU done (0.81–1.08) are the same in both. event → pump depends on each run's phase against the 1000 Hz events (p50 0.28–1.23 ms in both flavors, session 6's finding), so event → present (2.7–3.4 ms p50 in both) isn't compared. Every run's numbers: `build.noindex/s23/summary.txt` and `summary-trace.txt`.
  - **The traced runs** (`TRACE=1 LATSTATS=2`; the `.trace` files deleted): the window is never direct ("display rejected") and the display swaps at the game's rate (250/s), every presented handler with a time (status 2); **the Space goes direct on every swap, at 250/s**, in both layers. Exclusive and the borderless window go direct on every swap too, but at 218–222 swaps/s for 250 frames/s: ~12% of the frames never get a drawable, and their input is shown by the next frame (one frame later, ~4 ms: the p95 of ~6 ms). The probe's present times land on a display swap (within 2 µs) in every traced run (1452 of 1455 to 1676 of 1679); display_trace's frame-label check found 1 of them showing "that very frame" in SDL's exclusive run, ~all in the others (the times agree; not looked into).
  - **Exclusive fullscreen and the borderless window now lose frames, the Space doesn't** (both layers alike, so not a 6f question; for Phase 5). Session 6 measured exclusive at 2.07 / 2.26 / 2.41 with every frame shown, and session 19's no-input traces had native exclusive and the borderless window at 248 swaps/s. In this sitting the second display (the U4323QE at 60 Hz) swapped 60 times a second during every window, exclusive and borderless-window traced run, and not at all during the two Space runs (session 19's 248/s exclusive run: not at all either). The Claude app's window fills that display and redraws while a session works. So the loss goes with something drawing on the other display; not proven (Phase 5: the same runs with the second display idle and busy). The user plays in the Space (§3.1 #15), the clean mode: **2.15 / 2.4 / 2.5 ms**. *Explained and fixed in session 26 (the bullet "lost frames, and late presents" below): another application's frames on the other display make the WindowServer flip ours late; the Space blanks that display.*
- **The user's own mouse, `in_mouseRaw 1` (GCMouse) against `0` (the movement events, SDL's path)** (session 25, 2026-10-18, Phase 6 slice 6f's user half; the user at the machine moving their Model O3 Wired; unlocked, load 3.4–4.1). `MOUSE=0 port/latency.sh metal <name> 20 +set in_mouseRaw <1|0> +set r_fullscreen 1 +set r_noborder 1 +set r_customwidth 1920 +set r_customheight 1080` (the Space, `com_maxfps 250`, the 240 Hz G2524H), alternating 1, 0, 0, 1 (`build.noindex/s25/probe.sh`). The runs' whole-run percentiles, ms, p50 / p95 / p99:

  | Run | Frames with input | Shown by a later frame | newest event → pump | pump → present | newest event → present |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `in_mouseRaw 1`, run 1 | 5990 | 57 | 0.22 / 1.11 / 1.34 | 1.98 / 3.76 / 4.62 | 2.21 / 3.95 / 5.61 |
  | `in_mouseRaw 0`, run 1 | 5650 | 168 | 0.25 / 1.35 / 1.42 | 1.98 / 4.07 / 10.15 | 2.27 / 4.74 / 10.48 |
  | `in_mouseRaw 0`, run 2 | 5959 | 141 | 0.23 / 1.17 / 1.26 | 1.99 / 3.96 / 5.64 | 2.26 / 4.30 / 6.16 |

  - **The probe can't compare the two paths' input age:** its event time is the movement event's HID timestamp for `in_mouseRaw 0`, but the GCMouse handler's own clock read for 1 (GCMouse gives no HID time; `lastEventTimestamp` is wall-clock time at the handler, session 21). The handler runs a median 0.16–0.17 ms after the HID timestamp of the same movement (below), so raw 1's "newest event → pump" reads that much too low. Raw 1's p50 also moved from 0.22 to 0.65 ms between its runs: with GCMouse's ~800 calls/s and the 4 ms frame, the newest call's age at the pump depends on the phase between the mouse's polling and the frame clock, which drifts slowly within a run.
  - **Measured on one clock instead** (`build.noindex/s25/gcmpoll.m`: a window that captures the cursor as the game does and pumps the events every 4 ms as `MacOS_PumpEvents` does, recording every GCMouse call and every movement event; `build.noindex/s25/gcmlag.py` matches the two by position, strokes re-zeroed at rests; 18.4 s of the user's free movement: 15770 GCMouse calls, 859/s, and 4136 movement events, 225/s): at the 3532 pumps inside strokes **both paths show the same position at 96% of the pumps**; at 4% the movement events are ahead (GCMouse gets that report 0.06 ms p50, 1.09 ms p95 after the pump started: during the pump's own dequeue); GCMouse is never ahead. The input's age at the pump on the HID clock: movement events 0.59 / 1.63 / 3.66 ms (p50 / p95 / p99), GCMouse 0.59 / 1.65 / 3.66 (its handler time plus the 0.16 ms median handler lag). **So for the movement GCMouse is as fresh as the movement events at the game's pump, not fresher.** Likely why: the WindowServer coalesces new movement into the event still waiting in the queue, so the event the pump takes carries the newest report too. With an idle run loop instead (`build.noindex/s25/gcmlag.m`, the same recording under `[NSApp run]`) the events look 0.44–0.51 ms (p50) and 2.3–2.5 ms (p95) later than GCMouse: the main thread's own wake-up and dispatch, which a game that polls never pays. Session 21's ~1.8 ms earlier clicks were measured that idle way too; clicks in a polled loop weren't measured.
  - **What `in_mouseRaw 1` gives on the user's setup:** the same counts (the sums agree within a count: the user's movement events have no acceleration and no fractions) at the same time, and the raw counts whatever the system's pointer settings are (§3.1 #14). The pump → present spread of this sitting (p95 3.8–6.0 ms, 1–3% shown by a later frame) is wider than session 23's Space (2.4 ms, 0.1%) in both modes alike; the user and the session were active, the second display swapping (for Phase 5's lost-frames item, §6).
- **Exclusive fullscreen's and the borderless window's lost frames, and late presents** (session 26, 2026-10-18, Phase 5 slice 5d; the user's OK for the runs, nobody on the input, unlocked, load 2.6–6.6). No input (`MOUSE=0`), so instead of the probe a scratch log in the backend (never committed: `build.noindex/s26/tr_mtl-dlog.m`, `OMM_DLOG=<file>`) recorded every `nextDrawable` of the drawable thread, every frame's end and drawable, the presented handlers' times and the frame command buffers' GPU times; `build.noindex/s26/dl.py` computes **frame end → on screen**: the time from a frame's end (its drawable taken, ~0.2–0.3 ms after its pump) to the first frame at or after it that went on screen, the probe's attribution rule without input. dm/mohdm6, `com_maxfps 250`, vsync off, 2 frames in flight, the 240 Hz G2524H at 1920×1080, 16 s per run (`build.noindex/s26/run.sh <name> <excl|bwin|space|win> <none|cover|busy|small|gpuN>`; `none` = the 4K as it is, the Claude app's window filling it and redrawing at 60/s while the session works; `cover` = a still opaque window over the 4K, `busy` = the same changing color at 60/s (a full-screen composite per refresh), `small` = a moving square, `gpuN` = a `CAMetalLayer` window drawing N blended full-screen passes per refresh; `build.noindex/s26/d2.m`, `d2gpu.m`). Frame end → on screen, ms, p50 / p95 / p99, and the frames shown by a later one:

  | Mode, the 4K | 2 drawables, before | 3 drawables | **late presents** (5d) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | exclusive, the Claude app visible | 1.97 / 6.28 / 7.07 (13.2%); 2.01 / 6.00 / 6.64 (12.7%); 1.93 / 6.07 / 6.46 (11.3%); 2.00 / 5.99 / 6.69 (14.9%) | 1.97 / 4.62 / 5.43; 1.99 / 4.76 / 5.71 (0.9% presented, never shown) | **1.97 / 4.23 / 4.63 (0.1%); 1.99 / 4.35 / 4.76 (0%)**; traced 2.18 / 4.60 / 6.07 (0.8%) |
  | exclusive, the 4K covered | 1.95 / 2.31 / 2.53 (0.3%); 1.85 / 2.11 / 2.21 (0.1%) | 2.44 / 2.77 / 2.95 | 1.86 / 2.18 / 2.39 (0%) |
  | exclusive, `busy` / `small` | 2.40 / 2.70 / 2.96 (0.6%); 2.10 / 2.43 / 3.41 (0.6%) | | |
  | exclusive, `gpu20` (4.3 ms GPU per 4K frame) / `gpu100` (20.9 ms) | 2.53 / 8.24 / 10.75 (18%); 7.44 / 17.67 / 23.26 (46.5%) | | |
  | borderless window, the Claude app visible | 2.23 / 6.45 / 7.33 (13.8%); 1.91 / 5.83 / 6.50 (11.8%) | 2.00 / 4.86 / 6.17 (1.9% never shown) | **1.92 / 4.17 / 4.54 (0%)** |
  | borderless window, covered / `busy` | 2.15 / 2.39 / 2.55 (0.1%); 2.10 / 2.42 / 3.29 (0.3%) | | |
  | the Space, as it is (the 4K blanked by macOS) / covered / `busy` | 2.04 / 2.29 / 2.46 (0.4%); 1.87 / 2.00 / 2.19; 1.92 / 2.18 / 2.33 | 2.04 / **11.52** / 12.00 (1.5%) | 1.86 / 2.16 / 2.45 (0%) |
  | exclusive at `com_maxfps 1000`, covered / the Claude app visible | 2.50 / 2.90 / 3.60 (52%); 2.67 / 5.53 / 6.74 (62%) | | 2.48 / 2.93 / 3.62 (52%: none posted, below) |
  | window 1280×720, covered, 250 / 1000 fps | 2.44 / 4.70 / 10.49 (1.0%); 5.34 / 8.18 / 10.14 | | 250: 2.25 / 3.95 / 7.30 (0%, single runs: the compositor's mode varies); 1000: 5.52 / 8.07 / 10.52 |

  - **The cause:** the WindowServer holds our direct-to-display flip while it handles another application's frame on the other display. With the Claude app visible, the frame's GPU work is unchanged (commit → GPU start 0.39 / 0.47 ms p50 / p95, GPU work 0.40 / 0.47) but **GPU done → on screen goes from 1.08 / 1.31 ms (covered) to 1.16 / 3.81 ms**. With 2 drawables the next frame's drawable is the one still on screen until that late flip (it comes back 0.24–0.33 ms after its successor is on screen, in every run), so ~13% of frames find none and are skipped, and their input waits for the next frame (p95 ~6 ms). It isn't the 4K's composite as such (`busy` and `small` composite at 60/s and lose 0.6%) but the other application's GPU-rendered frames (the Claude app is Chromium; `gpu20` reproduces it, with GPU contention on top: our GPU work p95 4.6 ms). Game Mode was on in every exclusive run (gamepolicyd).
  - **Why the Space never lost frames:** this Mac has "Displays have separate Spaces" off (`defaults read com.apple.spaces spans-displays` = 1), so a fullscreen Space blanks the other display and the Claude app there stops drawing (session 23's Space runs: the 4K never swapped). In exclusive and the borderless window the 4K swapped 60/s in every traced run. Session 25's wider Space spread (p95 3.8–6.0 ms while the user was active) is not explained by this; see §6.
  - **3 drawables don't fix it:** every frame gets a drawable, but the flips still queue behind the late one (p95 4.6–4.9 ms), and when nothing is in the way they cost ~0.6 ms (p50 1.85 → 2.44; commit → GPU start 0.37 → 0.65 ms and GPU work 0.40 → 0.50: the GPU apparently clocked lower with more slack), as session 6 found; one Space run with 3 had p95 11.5 ms.
  - **Late presents (the fix, §3.2 "Added in session 26"):** a frame that ends without a drawable hands its final pass to the drawable thread, which presents it as soon as a drawable comes back. Its drawable came 0.34–0.47 ms (p50) and 1.6–3.1 ms (p95) after the frame's end, and a late present takes ~1.7 ms from its commit to the screen. **Every frame is now shown, and p95 drops from ~6.0 to ~4.2 ms** (p99 ~6.6 → ~4.7) in exclusive fullscreen and the borderless window while another application draws on the other display; nothing changes when nothing is in the way. The rest of the p95 is the late flip itself, which only the WindowServer controls (the Space avoids it by blanking the other display). A first version without a deadline was worse at 1000 fps (exclusive 2.50 / 2.90 → 3.76 / 5.81 ms; the window 5.34 → 6.35 p50): there the next frame is 1 ms away, and every drawable went to an older frame. With the deadline (a late present only if it can be committed 2 ms before the next frame's end) nothing is posted at 500 fps and above, and 1000 fps matches no late presents.
  - **Traced** (`LATSTATS=2 TRACE=1`, exclusive, the Claude app swapping the 4K at 60/s): the G2524H swapped 248 times a second (217 before), direct on every swap, every presented handler with a time; 1683 of the probe's 1689 present times inside the trace sit on a swap within 2 µs, 1679 showing that very frame; 36 of 4748 frames weren't presented. So the probe gets the late frames' present times, and they are real.
- **The GPU's wake-up before each frame** (session 28, 2026-10-18, Phase 5 slice 5f; unlocked, nobody on the input). No input (`MOUSE=0`), so every frame's commit, GPU-done and present times from `cl_latencyStats 2` (`build.noindex/s28/run.sh`, `fr.py`), dm/mohdm6, `com_maxfps 250`, vsync off, 2 frames in flight, the 240 Hz G2524H; `r_metalWakeGPU` 0 / 1 alternating, the same binary, 4 runs each in the Space, 2 in exclusive (the Claude app drawing on the 4K). µs, p50 / p95 / p99:

  | Mode | commit → GPU start (mean), 0 → 1 | commit → GPU done, 0 → **1** | commit → on screen, 0 → **1** |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | the Space | 597–642 → 351–360 | 1162 / 1336 / 1387 → **740 / 876 / 952** | 2359 / 2652 / 3528 → **2001 / 2305 / 2451** |
  | exclusive | 530–558 → 340–346 | 1074 / 1336 / 1404 → **730 / 897 / 1042** | 2382 / 4606 / 5353 → **2072 / 4529 / 5263** |

  - **The commit → GPU start gap of sessions 5–26 was the GPU waking up**: outside the game it grows with the idle time before the commit (0.12 ms at 1000 frames/s, 0.33 at 500, 0.57 at 250; `build.noindex/s28/gpustart`). The GPU sleeps after ~1.3–1.5 ms without work. A 4-byte blit committed when the frame starts (`re.WakeGPU`, §3.2 "Added in session 28") has it awake when the frame commits ~0.5 ms later: **every frame is on screen ~0.35 ms sooner at 250 fps** (p50 and p95). At ~1000 fps the GPU never sleeps (no change, nothing committed); with vsync the GPU is done sooner but the frame still waits for its refresh.
  - With session 23's Space numbers (pump → present 2.15 / 2.4 / 2.5 ms) this is ~1.8 ms pump → present; the probe shows it only with mouse input (the synthetic-mouse check, with the user).

At 250 both every 8 ms of game time; on the wall clock the old one's packets 7.5–8.5 ms apart, the new one's 8.0.

The old wait was one long coalesced `select` (up to ~10 ms late here). CPU 0.3 → 2.1% of a core (the polled last millisecond).

Every frame of every run on a performance core. The game clock is real time (×1.000 against the packets' arrival and the wall clock; uncapped, 81% of the frames are 0 ms).
  - **The first version, fixed in the session:** (1) the usercmd took `max(cl.serverTime, own time)`; at thousands of frames a second `cl.serverTime`'s clamp to the last drawn frame takes part of the −2 ms steps away (125 uncapped: 7 ms 2.7%, 6 ms 4.0% where stock has 6 ms 5.3%); (2) uncapped, a frame running across the usercmd's tick made it late by up to a frame's work: packets 69.7% within ±125 µs (p1 / p99 7703 / 8291); (3) render 250 with usercmds at 250 lost the phase between the two clocks after a restart: 823 usercmd-only frames (291 frames a second).

Rendering faster pumps faster, the key events carry their own millisecond, and a press inside a usercmd's window gives a fraction (31 / 63 / 95 at 250, 15…111 at 125; 2–6% of the usercmds), as stock's `CL_KeyState` does over a frame; the down time per press is kept (95–100 ms, most 96–97). `+use` taps of 3 ms: one usercmd each, every run.
  - **The view:** cgame's drawn view yaw minus the live yaw (`cl.viewangles`) on every drawn frame, within 0.01° of a constant on 100% of the frames in every mode; uncapped at 250 usercmds/s the view turned on 25077 of 31684 drawn frames that had no new usercmd (stock: on none, every frame is one).
  - **The server's aim** (MOHAA aims shots with the packet's eye angles, cgame's last drawn view, not the usercmd's angles): behind the packet's newest usercmd by one frame in stock (1.1° at 250, 2.25° at 125: ~4 and ~8 ms of turning), by ~0.2° at render 960 (~0.7 ms) and 0.1–0.2° uncapped (~0.1–0.3 ms).

    **Rendering faster doesn't bring the view to the screen sooner here:** at most ~470–500 frames a second reach the screen in the Space (the rest are replaced before they show; uncapped ~250), and pump → present is 0.4–0.5 ms longer at p50 and ~1.3 ms at p95 than stock 250. `r_metalLatePresent 0` changes nothing at 960 or uncapped (2.24 / 3.24 / 3.93, 2.34 / 3.53 / 3.80). **And the mode's frames have a slow tail even at render 250** (usercmds 125 or 250, display 0 or 1): pump → commit p95 0.55–0.56 ms against stock's 0.26; in its frame log 24% of the frames take ~550 µs instead of ~200, in stretches of consecutive frames (stock: 0.4%). Not explained yet (§6, session 32's first item). **Caveat: these probe runs used the instrumented `s31x` build, whose in-process server (single player) logged every packet with an `fflush` and traced the ground after every usercmd inside the game's frames (`s30log 1` doubled as its file name); the slow tail may be that, so session 32 first reruns them on the plain `metal` build.** *Corrected in session 32 (next entry): the tail was a bug of the mode (the server dropped every usercmd for ~10 s after the loading screen), not the instrumentation; and the per-frame times can't show what presenting more often gains: by the time on screen, render 480 / 960 shows input ~1.0 ms sooner than 250 and uncapped ~1.45 ms sooner.*

- **The decoupled mode's slow frames, and the render rate by the input's age on screen** (session 32, 2026-10-19, §4b part 2's follow-up; Phase 5 "Session 32 result"; the user's OK for the synthetic mouse, then away; the Space, 1920×1080 at 240 Hz, vsync off, `cl_cmdRate 125`, `cl_cmdDisplay 1`; every frame on a performance core in every run that logged them).
  - **The tail was the mode, not the instrumentation:** on the plain `metal` build the mode at render 250 still read pump → commit 0.22 / 0.56 / 0.58 and 0.20 / 0.55 / 0.57 against stock 250's 0.19 / 0.25 / 0.31 and 0.20 / 0.25 / 0.29. A scratch frame log with the frame's sections (`s32x`: IN_Frame, both event loops, `SV_Frame`, `CL_SendCmd`, BeginFrame, `CG_DrawActiveFrame`, the 2D, EndFrame) put all of it in cgame: `CG_DrawActiveFrame` ~400 µs instead of ~70 in 29–32% of the drawn frames, all in one block from the end of the loading screen to ~10 s later, while the in-process server took every packet and ran none of its usercmds (`SV_ClientThink` 0 times in 1566 of 1580 slow frames). So the snapshots' `commandTime` stood still and cgame's prediction replayed up to 64 usercmds every frame.
  - **Why:** the mode ran while the client was primed, before the first snapshot, when `cl.serverTimeDelta` is still 0. The usercmd made then stamped the next one with `cls.realtime` (10210 ms, the client's uptime) where the server's clock read 500; the loading screen paused the game, and the first usercmd after it took the client's real time (700): a step back of 9510 ms. A usercmd's time is sent as an 8-bit step from the one before when that step is below 256 (`MSG_WriteDeltaUsercmdKey`), and a step back is too, so the server read 10210 + 218 = 10428, kept it as its last usercmd and dropped every usercmd stamped below it, ~10 s of them. Online the same happens to a client whose uptime at the connection is ahead of a server's clock (a freshly started server): the player can't move for the difference.
  - **Fixed** (§3.2 "Added in session 32"): the mode starts with the first snapshot (`CA_ACTIVE`), and the next usercmd's time is never below the one before it (stock's "time never flows backwards"). After: pump → commit 0.19 / 0.23 / 0.28 against stock's 0.20 / 0.25 / 0.28; drawn work p50 / p99 199 / 279 µs (stock 208 / 290), no frame ≥ 400 µs; the server runs every usercmd from the first one.
  - **What presenting more often gains** (a new line of the probe, "input age on screen (by time)": the age of the newest mouse event in the image on screen, every 10 µs from one present to the next, while the mouse moves; and the mouse at 997 Hz, `MOUSEHZ=997`, so its events drift through every phase of the game's millisecond within each run: at exactly 1000 Hz newest event → pump sat at 0.44–1.26 ms for a whole run). ms p50 / p95 / p99, two runs each, alternating, plain `metal` after the fix:

    - **Rendering faster does show input sooner:** 480 and 960 by ~1.0 ms at p50 and ~1.8 ms at p95 against 250 (stock or the mode), uncapped by ~1.45 / 2.3 ms. 960 gains nothing over 480: about half its frames are replaced before they show (with 2 drawables the display takes ~470–540 images a second here). Session 31's "no gain" read the per-frame times, which leave out how long each image stays up: at 250 an image is up 4 ms, at 480 ~2.1 ms.
    - **Uncapped wins by ~0.4 ms over 480 / 960 because its GPU is never idle:** pump → GPU done 0.46–0.47 ms against 0.64–0.70 at 480 / 960 (commit → GPU done ~0.27 against ~0.45–0.5 ms). Two scratch prototypes (`s32x`, not kept) didn't get that at a lower rate: (1) a render frame that starts when the drawable thread holds a drawable (spinning until then, ~605 frames/s, 86% shown): 3.64 / 4.78 / 5.09 and 3.65 / 4.80 / 5.14, the same as 480; (2) keeping the GPU awake during the frame wait (the wake-up commit whenever it was idle 1 ms): 480 3.58 / 3.61, 960 3.60 / 3.57, the paced frames 3.61 / 3.60, stock 250 4.59 / 4.59, pump → GPU done unchanged (0.70 at 480): the GPU isn't asleep there, it is only faster under continuous load.

  - **JayRewd's breakage, reproduced in stock (rule 7):** at 500 and 1000 usercmds a second the server's own player leaves the ground for 1–27 ms where a slope changes and against the obstacle at the valley's top (B: 0.8–1.0% of the usercmds airborne, 5.5 landings a walk; 125: none), and a stock client at that rate plays it: 4.5–7.5 first-person landings a walk on B, 16–28 of them within 60 ms of each other (landing sounds, footsteps, the view bob and the weapon's offset stutter). 250 is between (1.5 server landings, 3.0 first-person). Speed barely changes (≤ 1.1%, on the steep slope at 1000), so "can't even walk on the terrain" didn't show on mohdm4's walkable terrain (≤ 36°): what shows is the stutter.
  - **The mode's server side is stock's at its command rate** in every run (the same lengths; B: no landings at 125, 2.5–3.0 at 250).
  - **Found and fixed (1): display 1's ground flicker.** The live usercmd is a pmove step of 1–7 ms, so cgame found the ground as a 1000-a-second client does: at 125 with render 480, 8.0 first-person landings a walk on B (30 changes within 60 ms), 12.5 / 48 at render 960, where stock 125 and display 2 play none; the server ran none of them. Fixed by keeping the ground from the newest usercmd's prediction after the live step (§3.2 "Added in session 33"): 0 / 0 on B at 480 and at 960, the whole runs 10 and 8 first-person landings against the server's 8; at 250, 3.0 / 10, stock 250's exactly.
  - **Found and fixed (2): display 1 predicted from a snapshot 50 ms old after any spawn or map load** (since session 30). `thisFrameTeleport` (set by a snapshot with `PMF_RESPAWNED`, and by `CG_ServerRestarted`) keeps the prediction on `cg.snap` instead of `cg.nextSnap` (and the solid list on its entities) until the replay of usercmds reaches the last frame's predicted time; in display 1 that time is the live usercmd's, between real usercmds, which the replay never reaches, so the flag stayed set for good unless two drawn frames in a row fell on usercmds. Seen first as late jumps (MOHAA's jump is the server's: `commanddelay 0.05 jump 56` in the state machine sets the velocity on a server frame, and the client gets it from the next snapshot): on mohdm6 with a jump tap every 737 ms (the phase against the server's 50 ms frames drifts), display 1 at 125 showed none of the 5-unit hops and every full jump 51 ms late in 6 of the 8 display-1 runs at 125 this session (cg.time − the predicted snapshot's command time p50 91–93 ms, against 41–43 in stock, display 2 and the other two). A scratch log of the snapshots sent and held (`inst33.py`: the server's `S` lines, cgame's `N` lines) showed the server and cgame's reading equal in all runs: the base was the old one. Fixed (§3.2): the replay compares against the time the last frame's live usercmd ran from. After, three runs: cg.time − the snapshot's command time p50 41–43 ms; every hop and jump shown at its take-off (first airborne frame's shown time − the take-off usercmd's p50 0–3 ms, p90 ≤ 8); first-person landings 20–21 against the server's 21–22, each at the server's usercmd (+0 / +1 ms), take-offs all within 8 ms. Stock 125: hops at 0 / 8 ms (one frame). Session 31's and 32's movement and latency numbers don't depend on the base snapshot (the replay reaches the same usercmd; the view takes the live angles).
  - **Ladders** (mohdm6's: origin 417 45 132, facing yaw 90; `s33lad.cfg`, a cycle of 8037 ms so the press drifts against the server's frames; eight cycles each of stock 125 and the mode at 125 with display 1 and 2): tele to its foot, walk to the wall, `+use`, 1 s up, 3 s back down and off at the bottom. Every cycle attached; the climb started 8–54 ms after the `+use` usercmd in every mode (the state machine acts on the server's 50 ms frames); up ~74.5 units/s, down ~80 in every mode; the same top (z 144, 160 when the phase gave one more 16-unit step); shown from snapshots for 3.15 s (3.30) in every mode, because a climbing player has `PMF_NO_PREDICTION` (`MOVECONTROL_CLIMBWALL`), so cgame shows the snapshots and the server's angles in every mode; prediction resumed at the bottom 0.03–0.06 units off the server's path (stock 0.04–0.06). A first schedule with 2.5 s up went over the top and got off it in all three modes. Not tried: getting on from the top.

GPU = the frame's command buffer, ms p50; "busy" = the union of the command buffers' GPU intervals over the segment; "slot" = the frame-slot wait, µs mean.

The alpha-tested draws (`r_s34list`: the static tree and bush models, one texture times vertex color, depth-writing, interleaved with the opaque draws; the world's treeline, hedgerow and grate cards with a lightmap) are **up to 75% of the GPU's time** (mohdm5 ~75%, obj_team1 up to 68%, mohdm4 22–50%, m2l1's tree view 34%): skipped, 960's GPU time falls to 0.20–0.25 on mohdm5, 0.18–0.43 on obj_team1, 0.26–0.42 on mohdm4.

  - **The prepass doesn't pay here** (the item asked in session 30: "will u test #3 to see if its worth it too?"). The prototype (`r_s34prepass 1`, JayRewd's form: per alpha-tested, depth-writing batch, first depth only with the alpha test, then shaded with depth EQUAL, no depth writes and no discard) costs mohdm5 +11–15% GPU time at 960 and −4 to −11% frames/s uncapped, obj_team2 +8–9% / −3 to −6%, and gains only on obj_team1 uncapped (+0–11%, the two-texture cards), ±4% there at 960. MOHAA's foliage shaders are one texture times the vertex color: the depth pass samples the same texture for every layer that the shaded pass did, so it saves only a multiply and the fog and adds a second pass. A whole-frame prepass has the same depth pass (every layer's alpha must be sampled before its depth is known), so it can save no more than the per-draw one; not prototyped.
  - **What does pay: the alpha-tested surfaces after the opaque ones** (`r_s34ftb 3`: after the sort, a stable partition of the `SS_OPAQUE` range puts every surface whose shader has an alpha-tested, depth-writing stage after the others; GL1's order is by shader, so the trees came before the terrain and the park's walls). Apple's GPUs drop hidden opaque fragments before shading, but an alpha-tested fragment is shaded before its depth is known, so trees drawn before what hides them were shaded whole. Drawn last, the finished opaque depth rejects their hidden fragments before shading. mohdm5 −8 to −26% GPU time at 960, uncapped +0 to +20% frames/s; obj_team2 uncapped +0 to +7%, at 960 −4% to +14% (0.346 → 0.396 in a view where the GPU is 35% busy and its clock governor sets the time); obj_team1 (measured with `r_s34ftb 2`), mohdm4 and m2l1 within ±4% at 960 and ±2% uncapped; slower uncapped in no view. Static models front to back within each shader (`r_s34ftb 1`) adds −1.5 to −4% on mohdm5, nothing elsewhere.
  - **The image** (`shotab.sh`: two runs of one cfg, `fixedtime 10`, 1280×720, no view model, the reorder off in one and on in the other, three captures a view): obj_team2 0, 9–10 (max 3), 15–16 (max 7) and 89–90 (max 17) pixels of 921,600; m2l1 13–14 (max 1; one capture had a single pixel at 51) and 0, the same pixels in all three captures: single pixels in the canopies and the fence, where an alpha-tested and an opaque surface meet at the same depth and the later draw wins (`LEQUAL`). mohdm5's snow differs from run to run (off against off: 25–270 pixels), so it can't be compared this way.
  - **Below 960 the GPU clocks down:** mohdm5 at render 480 GPU 0.84–1.23 ms a frame (busy 40–59%), at 250 0.98–1.07 (busy 25–27%), longer than at 960. With the reorder, one run: 480 0.840 → 1.177, 1.213 → 1.206, 1.233 → 1.029, 1.186 → 1.229 (the governor took the saved work as a lower clock in two views), 250 1.066 → 0.926, 0.983 → 0.677, 1.017 → 0.829, 0.993 → 0.931. Whether the reorder shows input sooner at capped rates needs repeated runs with the probe (commit → GPU done, the input's age on screen).

- **The alpha-tested surfaces after the opaque ones, in the shaders' order** (session 35, 2026-10-19; Phase 5 "Session 35 result", §3.2 "Added in session 35"; the user away, who asked for the session to run without them: HIDIdleTime 34 s at the start, two single touches by 13:03, then idle to the end; unlocked throughout). The committed form of session 34's `r_s34ftb 3`: an `SS_OPAQUE` shader with an alpha-tested, depth-writing stage (`alphaTestLast`) sorts after the other `SS_OPAQUE` ones in `tr.sortedShaders`, so every frame's radix sort puts its surfaces after the opaque surfaces at no per-frame cost. The A/B: session 34's frame log rebuilt on top of it as `s35x` (`build.noindex/s35/`: `inst35.py` adds `r_s35order 0` = GL1's order, re-sorting the shaders at the frame's start, and the sorts' CPU time per segment), session 34's setup (the Space at 1920×1080, 240 Hz, vsync off, the decoupled mode, single player, `noclip`, fixed views), per view and rate **A B A B** (old order, new, old, new; 1 s to settle, 2 s measured), mohdm5 and obj_team2 in two runs each (four reps), obj_team1, mohdm4 and m2l1 in one (two reps). **Cores: the main thread on a performance core at ≥ 98.7% of the commits in every segment** (100% in all but five). "commit → done" = the frame's commit to its command buffer's GPU end, p50: what the GPU adds to the frame's latency (it stands in for the probe, whose synthetic mouse needs the user).   - **The GPU's time per frame is lower or equal in every view at every rate but one** (obj_team1 yaw 0, +0.6% uncapped, both reps): mohdm5 −8 to −27% at 960 (commit → done −0.06 to −0.21 ms), uncapped +10 to +26% frames/s; obj_team2 and mohdm4 −0 to −13%; obj_team1 and m2l1 ±1–4%. At 960 on mohdm5 the CPU waits less for the GPU (frame-slot wait 28 → 8, 17 → 4, 20 → 3, 131 → 59 µs a frame). At 480 and 250 the governor clocks the GPU down (its time per frame is longer than at 960) and takes part of the saving as a lower clock, but the reps still agree within a few percent and nothing is slower.
  - **No CPU cost:** the order is fixed when a shader loads; the sort keys and batches are the same (the radix sorts' time per frame is equal with either order: mohdm5 24–41 µs, mohdm4 14–30, m2l1 2–10, the obj maps 1–6), and the CPU-bound uncapped views run as fast or faster (m2l1 2 4091 → 4126 frames/s, mohdm4 +1–5%). The bench: work equal (below).
  - **The image:** tie pixels only, where an alpha-tested and an opaque surface meet at the same depth and the later draw wins (`LEQUAL`). `port/shot.sh regress base-s29` with the new order (views numbered from 0, as `shot.sh` prints them): dm6 view 1 1 pixel (max 35), view 2 16 pixels in one of its three captures (max 27), dm4 views 0 / 1 / 3 8–9 / 22–25 / 46 pixels (max 2–3), obj3 view 0 3 pixels (max 4), m2l1 view 0 22–23 pixels (max 86; it varies by up to ~17 from run to run), of 921,600; the views that vary from run to run as always (dm6 view 8, dm5's snow, dm6fx views 4–7). The same run on `s35x` with `r_s35order 0` matches base-s29 in every other view exactly, so the toggle is GL1's order and the pixels are the order's. New baseline: `base-s35` (§3.1 #20).
  - **Static models front to back within a shader, on top** (`r_s34ftb 1` in `s35x`, A B A B at 960 and uncapped): mohdm5 GPU −0.3 to −3.6% at 960, uncapped +0.1 to +0.5% frames/s, for **3.3–4.1 µs of CPU a frame** (the sort per run of one shader's static models); mohdm4 ±0.6% GPU for 1.8–3.8 µs of CPU. Not taken: a cost on every map for a few percent on one.
  - **Found on the way:** the draw surfaces' radix sorts (`R_SortDrawSurfs`) take 24–41 µs a frame on mohdm5 and 14–30 on mohdm4 at 1920×1080 (up to four 8-bit passes over 16-byte `drawSurf_t`s; many static models' surfaces), a large share of a 960-a-second frame's CPU work there. A candidate (§6).

- **The draw surfaces' sort by runs of equal keys** (session 36, 2026-10-19; Phase 5 "Session 36 result", §3.2 "Added in session 36"; the user at the machine at the start (HIDIdleTime 1.7–20 s), "Later, not now" to the online test, the runs that take the cursor OK'd; unlocked throughout). Measured first with a scratch flavor `s36x` (`build.noindex/s36/`: b7f1729e's `R_RadixSort` kept beside the new one, `r_s36sort` 0 / 1 per segment, `r_s36check 1` sorting a copy with the old one too and comparing the bytes; per frame the sorts' µs, the surfaces, the key bytes that vary, the core and the frame's work, i.e. `Com_Frame` from the end of its wait to the next wait), session 35's setup and views (the Space at 1920×1080, the decoupled mode at 960, `noclip`), A B A B per view (1 s to settle, 2 s each), and each view's sort input dumped once (`r_s36keys`) for an offline benchmark of variants (`sortbench.c`, every variant checked against the old order).
  - **What the sort gets:** one `R_SortDrawSurfs` a frame on these views (no portals or mirrors), sprites only on m2l1 (0–18). The surfaces come in runs of equal keys in their input order (a BSP leaf's or a model's surfaces with one shader) and a frame has only a few hundred distinct keys: mohdm5 4,600–4,900 surfaces in 370–606 runs (8–13 a run, 260–310 distinct keys), mohdm4 1,900–3,500 in 830–1,750 (~2 a run), obj_team2 300–760 (1.6–2.3), obj_team1 57–307 (1.2–1.7), m2l1 310–1,260 (1.5). The key bits that vary are `0x3ff3ff00` on every map (the entity's low 10 bits, the static flag, 9 bits of the shader's sorted index): the dlight byte is constant, so the old sort ran three 8-bit passes. On the bench (mohdm6, 1280×720) 646 surfaces a frame (max 1,794) and 10 sprites, and all four bytes vary (dynamic lights).
  - **Why it was slow:** ~8.5 ns a surface (mohdm5 ~40 µs for 4,700), against ~2.4 ns offline on random keys of the same count. In a counting pass, neighbours that share a digit chain the counter's load, increment and store through memory, and here they nearly always do. Sorting 8-byte (key, index) pairs per surface (the brief's first idea) keeps the chain: offline 48 µs on mohdm5's input against the old sort's 32 (34 with 11-bit digits).
  - **The fix:** sort the runs instead (§3.2 "Added in session 36"). Offline on the dumped inputs: mohdm5 19–32 → 3.4–6.8 µs, mohdm4 12–21 → 6–11, the obj maps and m2l1 +3 to −40%; the same inputs shuffled (fewer runs) mohdm5 15–34 → 8–13; small inputs (4–64 surfaces) equal, 256–1,024 random or sprite-like keys +28–57% (up to +1.4 µs); all-distinct random keys +25–50% (9.6 → 12.5 µs at 4,096), an input this key layout doesn't produce (a few hundred distinct keys a frame).
  - **In the game, A B A B at 960** (the sort's µs a frame, mean (p50), old → new; the frame's work µs, mean; the main thread on a performance core in ≥ 99.9% of every segment's frames; runs from that view's dump):
