Of the frame-work samples, the backend is ~45%; what's left there is mostly fixed (encoders, commit, present: ~20% of the backend), the driver's per-draw cost (~15%), `RB_SkelMesh`'s LOD and indexes (~11%), `RB_Sphere_SetupEntity`'s sun traces (~7%) and the index copies.

Session 13 result (5a, the last pass, the user away): four more bit-identical CPU cuts, then **5a closed**.
- **Profile first** (`port/prof.sh`, 20 s of the looping timedemo, ~14,800 frames, screen unlocked; one sample ≈ one frame, so samples ÷ frames gives ms per frame; `sample` inflates the frame to ~0.28 ms). ms per frame:
  | Where | ms/frame |
  |---|---|
  | backend `RB_ExecuteRenderCommands` | 0.106 |
  | of it: draw submission (`RB_EndSurface` and below: the iterators, `mbDrawElements` 0.027, `MB_PrepareDraw` 0.014) | 0.049 |
  | of it: `RB_SkelMesh` 0.015, `RB_Sphere_SetupEntity` 0.0085 (sun traces 0.0036), `RB_SurfaceFace` 0.0048, `RB_StaticMesh` 0.0038, terrain 0.0033, `MTLimp_EndFrame` 0.0095, `MB_BeginPass` 0.0078 | |
  | front end `R_RenderView` | 0.048 |
  | of it: world 0.023 (the BSP walk 0.014, terrain 0.0045, static models 0.0026), skeletal surfaces 0.015 (the skeletor's bone transforms `TIKI_GetFrameInternal` 0.011), `R_RadixSort` 0.0086 | |
  | cgame's own work (`CG_DrawActiveFrame` minus `RE_RenderScene`) | 0.040 |
  | UI/2D widgets 0.014, `IN_Frame` (SDL pump) 0.010, OpenAL 0.003 | |
- **Change 1: the LOD-collapsed index lists are cached** (96829a23, `tr_model.cpp` `R_CollapsedIndexes`; §3.2 "Added in session 13"). `RB_SkelMesh` and `RB_StaticMesh` rebuilt each collapsed surface's index list every frame. Now it is built once per (surface, render_count) and copied with the base vertex added.
  - Reuse in the bench: 88% with the bounded table (95% unbounded, but the unbounded working set grows to ~14 MB over the demo: the timedemo moves entities a whole snapshot per frame).
  - Timed directly (temporary `mach_absolute_time` sums around the index building, 2 runs each, reverted): **13.5 → 8.9 µs per frame**. The bounded table (2 MB) beat the unbounded one (8.9 vs 9.5 µs): locality.
  - Bench, 15 runs each alternating in threes: metalref median 0.250 / mean 0.2503 (0.2475 without its first, warm-up run of 0.289), metal median 0.245 / mean 0.2473 ms. At the noise floor on its own; the combined bench below shows it with the others.
- **Change 2: the sun traces are reused** (6cb661ca, `tr_sphere_shade.cpp` `RB_Sphere_SunTrace`). ~10 sun traces per frame; 22% repeat within the frame (attachments share their root entity's lighting origin) and 50% repeat from earlier frames (entities that don't move). Profile: the traces under `RB_Sphere_SetupEntity` **3.6 → 2.3 µs per frame** (53 → 31 samples over ~14,000 frames).
- **Change 3: `R_RadixSort` in one counting pass, skipping constant bytes** (e777eb52, `tr_main.c`). The four counts in one pass; a byte that is equal in every key (the dlight byte without dlights) skips its scatter; fewer than 2 surfaces return at once (the sprite list, usually empty, paid four full passes). Profile: **8.6 → 5.2 µs per frame**.
- **Change 4: static models get their model-view matrix after culling** (f9aa7b47, `tr_staticmodels.cpp`). Every static model of the world had its 4×4 product computed before the cull (the PVS check is commented out upstream). The cull reads only the origin and axes, which are now copied first. The last model's rotation is completed after the loop, so `tr.ori` ends exactly as before. Profile: `R_RotateForStaticModel` in the front end **2.0–3.3 → 0.4 µs per frame**.
- **Combined bench**, same sitting, screen unlocked, 15 runs each alternating in threes:
  | Build | ms work per frame |
  |---|---|
  | metalref (2b3c6e0c, session 12) | mean 0.2476, median 0.250 (0.235–0.256) |
  | metal (the four changes) | mean **0.2334**, median **0.236** (0.222–0.239) |
  | GL1 | 0.948 / 0.949 / 0.953 |
  - **−0.014 ms per frame (−5.7%)**; every metal run is ≤ 0.239 ms. Metal does **4.1× less work per frame than GL1**.
- **Verification** (all four changes together; each is exact by construction):
  - `port/shot.sh regress base-s9`, and again with `+set r_metalStaticWorld 2`: identical to the Metal baseline except the views that vary from run to run (dm6 view 8, 1998–1999 px; dm5's snow, 11–70 px; dm6fx views 4–7; m2l1 view 0, 1 and 17 px at (753,374)). dm6 views 0–7, dm6fx 0–3, dm4 and obj3 identical. Against GL1 the same numbers as sessions 9–12: dm6 1454 / 135 / 496 / 2533 / 1404 / 152 / 117 / 1 / 2437, dm6fx 0 / 4 / 605 / 899882 (view 3, also session 12's), m2l1 4252–4260 / 753, dm4 843 / 1694 / 323 / 521, obj3 11389 / 16 / 14151.
  - `port/skinab.sh` (build "installed"), whole bench: 99,506 batches, 19.6 M vertices, **0 differences in every field** (the same counts as session 11).
  - The build passes; `port/glcheck.sh` is clean.
- **Looked at and left:**
  - the world's BSP walk (`R_RecursiveWorldNode`, 0.014 ms): classic Q3 code, everything inlined, memory-bound; omaha's exact world culls (§4b: flat PVS leaf list, 8-leaf group culls, per-face bounding spheres) are the candidates, kept for later with a screenshot diff each;
  - the skeletor's bone transforms (`TIKI_GetFrameInternal`, 0.011 ms): shared with the game code, outside the renderer;
  - `RB_SkelMesh`'s mesh search (a `TIKI_GetSkel` call and a list walk per surface; omaha's "mesh-index table at model load"): a few µs at most.

**Phase 5a, closed (session 13).** The ≤ 0.25 ms target is met: **0.234 ms** work per frame unlocked (median 0.236), down from 0.41 at the start of 5a, against GL1's 0.95 in the same sitting. The GPU is ~10% busy (~0.1 ms per frame): the frame is CPU-bound. Where the remaining ~0.23 ms goes (session 13's last profile, `s13all2`: 3140 frame-work samples over ~13,500 frames ≈ 0.232 ms per frame, the bench's figure):
- **Metal and the driver, ~17%** (+3% ring copies): AGX, IOGPU/IOKit, the kernel and objc under the backend. That is the per-draw state emission in `drawIndexedPrimitives` (~58 draws per frame) and the fixed per-frame costs (`MB_BeginPass`: command buffer and scene encoder; `mbEndFrame`: commit, the final pass, present, the frame-slot wait; together ~7%). Fewer draws means merging batches, which changes the draw order (not bit-identical). The fixed costs come with the frame structure.
- **Engine-side, ~42%:** cgame's own work (~18%), the UI widgets, the client frame, the SDL pump and OpenAL (~19%), and the skeletor's animation called from the front end (`TIKI_GetFrameInternal`, ~5%). None of it is renderer code.
- **Renderer CPU, ~38%:** the front end without the skeletor (~17%: world walk, entity surfaces, sort) and the backend's own code (~21%: the surface functions, the iterators, `MB_PrepareDraw`). What was cheap to cut exactly has been cut; omaha's exact world culls are the next candidates (above).

Session 14 result (5b, p99 frame time vs GL1, the user away):
- **The per-frame work log** (ae894bcf, client only, timedemo only): `CL_SetCGameTime` records each timedemo frame's work, µs since the last timedemo frame minus the frame limiter's wait added since then (`com_timedemoWaitUsec`), in `clc.timeDemoWorkUsec` next to `timeDemoDurations`. `cl_timedemoLog` writes it as a second column after a `# frame ms, frame work usec` comment line. The mean of the column matches the "timedemo work" figure (0.2357 vs 0.236 ms). No effect on pacing or usercmds: one `Com_Microseconds()` per timedemo frame.
- **`port/bench.sh run`** prints p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max of the work µs (nearest rank) at the end of each run line, and a last line over all the runs of the call.
- **The `gl` reference carries the log:** `port/build.sh gl` applies the client hunks of `GL1_PATCHES` (ae894bcf) to the `GL1_REF` export (c9dddadd, unchanged); the export is redone when the patch list changes (`.gl1-ref` holds both). The renderer of the reference is untouched.
- **Sitting** (screen unlocked throughout; Metal at ae894bcf and GL1, alternating one run each, 8 runs each; 2397 logged frames per run). Work µs per frame:
  | Build | p50 | p95 | **p99** | **p99.9** | max | mean work |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Metal, pooled | 212 | 454 | **739** | **1536** | 7880 | 0.237 ms |
  | GL1, pooled | 848 | 1681 | **2318** | **3490** | 10251 | 0.914 ms |
  - Per run: Metal p99 671 / 688 / 670 / 773 / 669 / 1005 / 859 / 709, p99.9 1341 / 1391 / 1200 / 2453 / 1218 / 1578 / 1601 / 1073; GL1 p99 2503 / 2024 / 2433 / 2363 / 1910 / 2469 / 2031 / 2702, p99.9 3375 / 2695 / 3370 / 3570 / 2863 / 3541 / 3013 / 3833. The worst Metal run's p99 (1005 µs) is below GL1's best (1910 µs) and near GL1's median frame (848 µs). Metal's pooled p99 is **3.1× lower** than GL1's, its p99.9 2.3× lower.
  - Logs: `build.noindex/s14/sit1/` (`summary.txt`, the per-run frame logs and game logs).
- **The slow frames** (temporary timers around the client frame's parts, cgame's calls into sound/registration/traces, the renderer's front end/backend, pipeline and image creation, and file opens; never committed, kept as `build.noindex/s14/diag.patch`):
  - **The frames that are slow in every run** (1343, 1302, 2252–2253, 2392, 713, 296, 318, …: +0.6–1.0 ms on Metal, +0.7–1.2 ms on GL1, at the same frames) **are failed sound opens.** The demo triggers ~83 dialogue lines per run that aren't in the retail data (`sound/dialogue/generic/a/death/dpl_death_02i.wav`, `…/damage/dpl_damage_12i.wav`, …; GL1 logs the same 83 `WARNING: Failed to open sound`). Each trigger costs 250–400 µs on the main thread: `S_OPENAL_StartSound` → `openal_channel_two_d_stream::set_sfx` → `S_CodecGetSound` tries the WAV, MP3, OGG and Opus codecs in turn, each `FS_FOpenFileRead` misses in the two directory search paths, and each miss runs `FS_CorrectCase` (`access` + `opendir`/`readdir` of the parent), then the warning is printed. Nothing remembers the failure. Both volumes are case-insensitive APFS, so `FS_CorrectCase`'s directory scan can never find anything there. 20 of the 23 frames above one run's p99 had ≥ 150 µs in cgame's `S_StartSound`; server-sent sounds hit the same path from the demo parse (`demo` 266–521 µs at frames 296, 864, 2252). Engine-side (client sound code and `qcommon/files.cpp`), not renderer: the follow-up is in §6.
  - **Frame 0** (5–8 ms Metal, 3–10 ms GL1): the first timedemo frame. ~5 ms is `IN_Frame` + `Com_EventLoop` (the first in-game frame after the load), ~0.4 ms the first draw's registrations (`global/lensflaredefs.txt`, a HUD texture, 3 shaders).
  - **Metal-side:** an occasional explosion frame (713–715 in some runs) shows a front-end spike (0.45 ms) and then a frame-slot wait in `RE_EndFrame` (0.2–1.1 ms): the GPU falling two frames behind on a heavy-overdraw frame. `r_metalPresentStats`: ~0.001 ms of slot wait per frame on average, 0 ms waiting for drawables. No pipeline was created and no image uploaded during the timedemo (first-use costs are all at load). The other p99.9 frames (e.g. 1087–1093 in one run, 4.4 / 2.3 / 2.2 ms) are not repeatable: scheduling noise.
  - In MOHAA the 2D draws flush the pending render commands (`Draw_StretchPic` → `R_IssuePendingRenderCommands`), so the 3D backend runs inside cgame's draw, and `RE_EndFrame` only presents (~5–15 µs).
- **Optional world culls, measured and skipped:** the bench adds ~478 world faces per world walk, ~119 of them (25%) entirely outside the frustum by their point bounds (temporary counter, reverted). A per-face cull would save their sort and backend work but costs a sphere test on all 478; the estimate is ≤ ~2 µs net per frame, under what the bench resolves, and the whole BSP walk is 14 µs. omaha's flat leaf list / 8-leaf group culls bound the gain at a few µs more. Not done; the sound fix above is worth ~100× more at p99.
- Build passes; `port/glcheck.sh` clean. No renderer code changed (so no regress/skinab run needed).

Session 14, continued: **5c, the engine-side p99 tail** (the user moved it ahead of Phase 7, §3.1 #8). Both changes are outside the renderer and keep the output the same:
- **Confirmed:** the dialogue lines are missing from the retail data. `sound/dialogue/Generic/A/damage/` in Pak4 holds only `dpl_damage_13i.wav`, and there are no `death` files at all; the aliases reference more variants than the game ships, so the original game fails on them too. Streamed sounds are never checked at registration (`S_LoadSound` returns at once for them), so every start opened the file.
- **Change 1** (9e1c543f, `snd_openal_new.cpp`, `snd_dma_new.cpp`, `snd_local_new.h`): `openal_channel_two_d_stream::set_sfx` marks the sfx `SFX_FLAG_STREAM_FAILED` when `S_CodecLoad` fails, and returns at once on later starts (with the same side effects: `stop()`, the channel fields). `S_BeginRegistration` clears the flag on every sfx, so each map load (after any download) searches again. cgame calls `S_RegisterSound` on every trigger, so the flag can't be reset there. Warnings per run: 83 → 29 (once per missing file per map).
- **Change 2** (0d0b3148, `qcommon/files.cpp` `check_filecase`): every file read from a pk3 first misses in the two directory search paths, and each miss ran `FS_CorrectCase`: `access()` on every path component, then an `opendir`/`readdir` scan of the first missing component's parent. On a case-insensitive volume `access()` already ignores case, so the scan can't find anything: `check_filecase` now asks that directory's own volume (`pathconf(dir, _PC_CASE_SENSITIVE)`) and skips the scan when it says 0. Exact across mount points; case-sensitive volumes still scan. It also speeds up every streamed sound that does exist (each start opens its file).
- **Bench** (screen unlocked; work µs per frame, pooled):
  | Sitting | Build | p50 | p95 | **p99** | **p99.9** | mean work | wall per run |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Session 26 result (5d, exclusive fullscreen's and the borderless window's lost frames; the user at the machine at the start, asked: "Go ahead" for the runs that take the cursor, "Lost frames, no me"; then away, the screen locked once after ~20 min without input and the user unlocked it; commit on `metal`, not pushed: Phase 5 isn't done):
- **Reproduced and explained** (§2, "lost frames, and late presents"): with another application drawing on the other display (the Claude app on the 4K, redrawing at 60/s while a session works), the WindowServer flips our direct-to-display frames late (GPU done → on screen p95 1.3 → 3.8 ms), the next frame's drawable is still on screen, and ~13% of frames are skipped (their input shown a frame later: p95 ~6 ms). A still window over the 4K, or my own 60 Hz layer animation there, removes it; GPU-rendered frames there cause it (`d2gpu`). The Space never showed it because macOS blanks the other display for a fullscreen Space on this Mac ("Displays have separate Spaces" off). Game Mode was on throughout.
- **3 drawables rejected** again: the flips still queue (p95 4.6–4.9), and +0.6 ms p50 when nothing is in the way.
- **Late presents** (`r_metalLatePresent`, default 1; §3.2 "Added in session 26"; `tr_mtl.m`: `MB_EncodeFinal`, `MB_PostLatePresent`, `MB_PresentLate`, `MB_CancelLatePresent`, the drawable thread): a frame without a drawable is presented by the drawable thread as soon as one comes back, if it can still be committed 2 ms before the next frame's end. Exclusive with the Claude app visible: **1.97 / 4.23 / 4.63 and 1.99 / 4.35 / 4.76 ms** (frame end → on screen p50 / p95 / p99; before 1.93–2.01 / 5.99–6.28 / 6.46–7.07), 0–0.1% of frames shown by a later one (before 11–15%); the borderless window 1.92 / 4.17 / 4.54 (before 1.91–2.23 / 5.83–6.45 / 6.50–7.33); the clean cases and 1000 fps unchanged. A traced run puts the probe's present times on the display's swaps (1683 of 1689 within 2 µs; 248 swaps/s instead of 217).
- **Bench, ABBA ×2 each** (`build.noindex/s26/abL.sh` / `abL2.sh`: the same install with `r_metalLatePresent 0` and `1`; unlocked; the window bench at ~1000 fps): the first version without the deadline (late presents on ~40% of the bench's frames): off 0.228 / 0.238 / 0.225 / 0.213 ms work (mean 0.226), pooled p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 215/387/513/1070/4626 µs; on 0.225 / 0.246 / 0.229 / 0.219 (0.230), 216/392/533/1107/7002. The final version (13–16 late presents per run): **on 0.221 / 0.223 / 0.215 / 0.223 (0.2205), 210/367/487/776/5443 µs; off 0.222 / 0.224 / 0.222 / 0.225 (0.223), 212/372/492/707/6919: equal.** (The scratch log was compiled in, inactive, for these; the final binary differs only by its removal.)
- **Checks:** build; `port/glcheck.sh` clean; the seal verifies; the final binary in exclusive with the Claude app visible (1286 frames without a drawable at their end, 1203 presented late) and with vsync (`r_swapInterval 1`: 3 drawables, every frame presented, no late presents). No rendering change (the final pass is the same pass, moved into `MB_EncodeFinal`), so no fixed views.
- **Tools** (scratch, `build.noindex/s26/`): `run.sh` (one `MOUSE=0` run: mode × what is on the 4K, the scratch log analysed by `dl.py`), `batch.sh` (several, stops when the screen is locked or the input was used), `d2.m`/`d2gpu.m` (the 4K: `cover`, `busy`, `small`, `gpuN`), `dl.py` (frame end → on screen, the skipped frames' drawable delay, the drawable's return after its successor's flip, commit → GPU → screen), `tr_mtl-dlog.m` (the backend with the scratch log, `OMM_DLOG=<file>`: the prototype before the deadline and the final polish; diff it against `tr_mtl.m` to carry the log over), `abL.sh`/`abL2.sh` (ABBA bench of a cvar).

Session 27 result (5e, the pipeline items; the user away at the start (8 min idle), back once mid-session and asked before the bench: "Run them now" for the runs that take the cursor, "Not now" for the parts that need them; commits on `metal`, not pushed: Phase 5 isn't done):
- **The pipeline cache was mostly broken, and silently.** `serializeToURL:` fails ("Failed to generate machO … expecting 'fragment' stage in pipeline no. N") on an archive that was read from a file and then got pipelines added, for some sets of pipelines: reproduced outside the game (`build.noindex/s27/archtest`): an archive of `final` + `0x0e001` read back, plus `0x0e089` or `0x0e005`, fails; plus `0x0e003` or `0x2e001` works; a dozen additions failed every time; with or without the depth query pipeline. After a failed serialize, the same archive object writes only what was added after the failure (session 19's "1 pipeline instead of 13"). This client routes the renderer's `PRINT_WARNING` to `Com_DPrintf` (developer only), so nobody saw it: every bench run in session 26's logs found 2 of its 15 listed pipelines in the archive and compiled the other 13 at startup (cheap only because the system shader cache had them). `metalref` shows it plainly: a menu-only run, then the bench twice: "pipeline cache not written" on both (with `developer 1`), the archive stuck at the menu's pipelines, 10 created at their first draw on every run.
- **A fresh archive always serializes** (archtest: 5, 24 and 45 pipelines, the query among them, every one found after reading it back). So the cache now writes a new archive holding every pipeline the process created (their descriptors are kept), never the one it read; only when the run compiled a pipeline (`mb_archiveDirty`), ~30 ms at the quit or `vid_restart`. A failed write leaves the old archive and prints a visible line; the keys file is written whenever the list grew, even then (a listed key the archive lacks is compiled up front by the next run, never in play). `MB_CACHE_VERSION` 2, now part of the file names' hash, retires version 1's archives. **The query pipeline stays in the archive:** a fresh archive with it reads back fine, and the failure came with or without it.
- **Pipelines ahead of their first draw** (`R_PrecacheShaderPipelines` in `FinishShader`, `R_PrecacheState` next to `R_State`, `mbPrecachePipeline` / `mbPrecacheFinish` in the backend): each shader, when it is loaded, describes the state each of its stages draws with, as the stage iterators set it up: the generic iterator per stage (blend, alpha test, one or two texture units), the vertex-lit fast path also with GPU skinning, the lightmapped multitexture path, the sky's outer box; plus ProjectDlightTexture's two passes (models under dynamic lights) at startup. The backend takes a missing pipeline from the archive at once, else compiles it with `newRenderPipelineStateWithDescriptor:completionHandler:` on Metal's threads (up to 64 at a time). A draw that needs one still compiling waits for that one only; `RE_EndRegistration` (the end of `CL_InitCGame`) waits for the rest, so none is compiled in play. The startup's keys list goes the same way (its misses compile in parallel). `R_State` is unchanged: a first version moved its blend switch into a helper, which stayed an out-of-line call per blend change, so the factors became a table in `R_PrecacheState`.
- **Measured:**
  - archtest, variants the system shader cache had never seen: 5–30 ms each one at a time on the calling thread; 8 at once with the async call: submitted in ~2 ms, all done 37 ms later on Metal's threads. A process's first pipeline call costs ~10 ms (the compiler service's connection), the first specialization of `mb_stage_fs` ~10 ms.
  - Empty cache, the bench demo (dm6): 16 of 17 pipelines compiled ahead of their first draw (the slowest done 0.6 ms after its submission; 0.0 ms waited at the end of the load). The one created at its first draw is `0xe548`, the console's untextured box (`DrawBox`; the console and menu only, never predicted; the keys list has it after one run). Before (`metalref`): all 14 at their first draw.
  - Warm: 17 of 17 listed pipelines from the archive at startup in 1.2 ms (before: 2 of 15, the rest compiled), none compiled ahead or at a first draw, nothing written. A menu-only archive (6) + the bench map (11 new): written, the next run 17 of 17 from the archive. dm/mohdm4 after dm6: 1 new variant compiled ahead (0.4 ms, nothing waited), written, the next run 18 of 18; m2l1 and obj/obj_team3 after that brought nothing new.
  - The bench's keys: `0e001 0e003 0e005 0e007 0e089 0e119 0e11b 0e149 0e14b 0e189 0e309 0e459 0e548 0e549 0e54b 2e001 2e005` (`0e11b` and `0e14b`, two multitextured blends, are predicted but not drawn there).
- **Bench, ABBA ×4** (`build.noindex/s27/ab.sh`, each flavor with its own pipeline cache: `metal` and `metalref` delete each other's files; `metalref` = ff5691e2, the session's start; unlocked, nobody on the input, load 2.5–4.2): `metal` 0.217 / 0.215 / 0.218 / 0.216 / 0.217 / 0.218 / 0.217 / 0.217 ms work (mean 0.2169), pooled p50/p95/p99/p99.9 207/359/470/667 µs; `metalref` 0.214 / 0.221 / 0.214 / 0.218 / 0.217 / 0.217 / 0.215 / 0.217 (mean 0.2166), 207/362/479/623 µs. **Equal.** (The first version, with the helper in `R_State`, 3 rounds earlier: 0.2188 vs 0.2168 over 12 runs each, within the noise; the helper went anyway.)
- **Checks:** build (the install signs and checks the seal); `port/glcheck.sh` clean; renderer warnings none new. Rendering can't change (the same descriptors, created earlier), so no fixed views.
- **Tools** (scratch, `build.noindex/s27/`): `archtest.m` (the archive outside the game: `open <file|new>`, `get <key>`, `query`, `final`, `save <file>` (version 1's write), `fresh <file>` (a new archive of everything created), `async <keys> end`), `ab.sh` (ABBA bench, one pipeline cache per flavor in `cache/<flavor>/`, numbering continued from the files in `ab/`).

Session 28 result (5f, the GPU's wake-up before each frame; the user at the machine at the start, asked: "Run them now" for the runs that take the cursor, "Not now" for their parts; then away, the screen locked ~20 min after their last input, asked again: "Unlocked, run them"; commits on `metal`, not pushed: Phase 5 isn't done):
- **What the ~0.37 ms from commit to GPU start is: the GPU powering up after idling.** Outside the game (`build.noindex/s28/gpustart`: no window; per period one command buffer, a 1920×1080 render pass with ~0.16 ms of GPU work, committed 0.25 ms after the "frame start", 2 in flight; paced exactly by sleeping to 1.5 ms before the start and spinning, because a sleeping thread woke up to ~0.8 ms late): commit → GPU start p50 **0.12 ms at a 1 ms period, 0.33 ms at 2 ms, 0.57 ms at 4 ms, 0.58 at 8 ms** (p95 0.17 / 0.40 / 0.70 / 0.72); the minimum ~0.06–0.1 ms at every period. After ~1.3–1.5 ms without work the GPU goes to sleep, and the next commit waits ~0.3–0.5 ms for it. The bench never shows it (~1000 fps: 0.124 ms in `r_metalPresentStats`); at 250 fps every frame paid it (in play, the Space: 0.60–0.64 ms mean).
- **A tiny command buffer ahead of the frame wakes it.** gpustart at 4 ms, the frame's GPU end in µs after its start (committed at ~260): no kick ~935–1019; a blit committed at the frame's start ~750; 0.3 ms before it ~680; 0.8 ms ~640; 1.0 ms ~612; 1.3 ms ~590; 1.6 ms ~780; 2 ms ~878 (the GPU has gone back to sleep). The kick's own commit → start is ~0.5 ms (it pays the wake-up instead of the frame). Blit, compute and render kicks do the same; an empty command buffer never reaches the GPU ("done" in 0.3 µs) and wakes nothing. A kick costs 16–33 µs of CPU (p50), a process's first one 0.4–1.2 ms (the driver's first blit or command setup).
- **In the game** (`re.WakeGPU`, §3.2 "Added in session 28"): the renderer commits a 4-byte blit fill when the engine starts a frame, unless something was committed within the last millisecond. Placement, measured with temporary counters (`build.noindex/s28/dbg.patch`, never committed; GPU start after commit by the kick's lead before the frame's commit, the Space at 250 fps): lead 0.2–0.3 ms → 0.35 ms; 0.4–0.8 ms → 0.23–0.28; 0.9–1.6 ms → 0.29–0.38; 1.7–2.0 ms → 0.37–0.55 (dozing again); no kick 0.66. A first version kicked inside the frame limiter's wait, in its last millisecond: only 46% of the frames got one (the limiter's 3 ms `select` often wakes past the frame's tick, and its last-millisecond branch never runs), and the ones that did came 1.4–1.9 ms ahead of the commit. A kick right after the wait, before `IN_Frame`, lands 0.5–0.6 ms ahead (the frame's pump → commit) on every frame: the best spot, and one call site.
- **Measured** (`build.noindex/s28/run.sh`: `MOUSE=0 LATSTATS=2` runs of `port/latency.sh`, dm/mohdm6, `com_maxfps 250`, vsync off, 15 s; `fr.py` pools every frame's commit, GPU-done and present times from `cl_latencyStats 2`; `r_metalWakeGPU` 0/1 alternating, the same binary; unlocked, nobody on the input, load 2.1–4.7). µs, p50 / p95 / p99, 4 runs each pooled (19 k frames) in the Space, 2 each in exclusive:

  | Mode | `r_metalWakeGPU` | commit → GPU start (mean) | commit → GPU done | commit → on screen |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | the Space | 0 | 597–642 | 1162 / 1336 / 1387 | 2359 / 2652 / 3528 |
  | the Space | **1** | **351–360** | **740 / 876 / 952** | **2001 / 2305 / 2451** |
  | exclusive, the Claude app on the 4K | 0 | 530–558 | 1074 / 1336 / 1404 | 2382 / 4606 / 5353 |
  | exclusive, the Claude app on the 4K | **1** | **340–346** | **730 / 897 / 1042** | **2072 / 4529 / 5263** |
  | the Space, `com_maxfps 0` (~1000 fps) | 0 / 1 | 133 / 130 | 506 / 543 / 589; 502 / 540 / 590 | 1614 / 1866 / 1966; 1618 / 1878 / 1962 (10 wake-ups in the run) |
  | the Space, vsync (`r_swapInterval 1`), 1 run each | 0 / 1 | 734 / 412 | 1226 / 1369 / 1393; 757 / 860 / 1206 | 6529 / 7716 / 7777; 6537 / 7702 / 7761 |

  - **The frame is on screen ~0.35 ms sooner at 250 fps** (p50 and p95, the Space and exclusive alike), and its GPU work is done ~0.42 ms sooner. Every run with it was below every run without it. The GPU's own work also got shorter (0.45–0.46 vs 0.46–0.48 ms per frame). The commit intervals are unchanged (p50 4.01 vs 4.03 ms): pacing and input sampling are untouched; the call costs ~20–30 µs, between the wait and `IN_Frame`.
  - At ~1000 fps the GPU never sleeps and nothing is committed (10 wake-ups in a run, at the load). With vsync the GPU is done sooner, but the frame still waits for its refresh (the vsync queue: the low-latency vsync item of §4b).
  - From session 23's Space numbers (pump → present 2.15 / 2.4 / 2.5 ms), this puts pump → present at ~1.8 ms; the probe needs mouse input to show it (the synthetic-mouse check, with the user).
- **Bench** (`build.noindex/s28/ab.sh`, ABBA ×4 twice; `metalref` = 824f3acd, the session's start; unlocked, nobody on the input, load 2.4–3.8), ms work per frame and pooled p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max µs:
  - first set: `metal` 0.225 (its first run after a build) / 0.218 / 0.216 / 0.219, 207/374/501/1367/7573; `metalref` 0.214 / 0.215 / 0.216 / 0.215, 205/360/470/651/5792. `metal`'s first frame was 6.0–7.6 ms against 4.1–5.8: a bench run makes one wake-up, at its first frame after the load, and it was the process's first blit (0.4–1.2 ms in gpustart). So the renderer now commits one when it starts.
  - second set, with that: `metal` 0.218 / 0.220 / 0.226 / 0.217 (mean 0.2203), 210/370/490/909/5595; `metalref` 0.215 / 0.215 / 0.220 / 0.217 (0.2168), 207/357/476/864/7466. First frames 2.9–5.6 vs 4.4–7.5 ms.
  - the same binary with `r_metalWakeGPU` 0 / 1 / 1 / 0: 0.215 / 0.218 / 0.216 / 0.214; pooled 0: 204/360/469/723, 1: 207/362/474/661.
  - Without frame 0 (µs per frame): `metal` 215.6 (runs 2–8 without one 224.5), `metalref` 213.2 (without one 218.4); wake 0 vs 1: 213.0 vs 214.8. **+~2 µs (1%), inside the runs' spread** (212–218 for one build): the bench makes one wake-up per run, and the per-frame check is a clock read and a compare. p99 equal. Not chased further; watch it in the next bench.
- **Checks:** build (game, `omohaaded`; the install signs and checks the seal); `port/glcheck.sh` clean. No rendering change (a blit into the renderer's own 16-byte buffer), so no fixed views.
- **Tools** (scratch, `build.noindex/s28/`): `gpustart.m` (commit → GPU start outside the game: `gpustart <period_us> <frames> <cpu_us> <draws> <none|empty|blit|compute|render> [lead_us]`, prints percentiles, the timeline's medians and the kick's CPU cost; build line in its header), `run.sh <name> <space|excl|win> <r_metalWakeGPU> [engine args]` (one `MOUSE=0 LATSTATS=2` run with `r_metalPresentStats`), `fr.py <logs>` (pooled per-frame commit → GPU done / on screen / commit intervals), `ab.sh` (session 27's, with this session's `ab/` and `cache/`), `dbg.patch` / `tr_mtl-dbg.m` (the temporary counters: GPU start by kick lead, kicked or not, with or without a drawable), `batch.sh` / `batch2.sh` and their outputs.

Session 29 result (§4b part 1, the µs frame limiter; the user at the machine at the start, asked: "Run them now" for the runs that take the cursor; away ~45 min during the runs, back for the decision: §3.1 #19, and "Yes, run them" for the synthetic mouse; commits on `metal`, not pushed: Phase 5 isn't done):
- **The wait, measured outside the game** (`build.noindex/s29/waittest.m`: a frame loop of busy work, a sleep to a margin before the deadline, a spin to it; this M4's CPUs 0–5 are efficiency cores, 6–9 performance, `ioreg` `cluster-type`):
  - `select` and `mach_wait_until` wake late by ~20–25% of their timeout, at the default QoS and at `USER_INTERACTIVE` alike (timer coalescing): a 0.6 / 1.6 / 2.6 / 3.6 ms sleep 0.13 / 0.33 / 0.53 / 0.73 ms late; a 25–50 ms `select` 3.5–5 ms. `NSActivityLatencyCritical` changes nothing. `kevent` with a timeout: ~13%.
  - A kqueue EVFILT_TIMER with `NOTE_CRITICAL | NOTE_LEEWAY` 0: ~25 µs late at p50, ~52–58 at p99, ~100–300 at p99.9, at any length (0.3 to 50 ms). Re-adding the oneshot timer clears a firing that happened while nobody waited (`kqstale.c`).
  - `gettimeofday` (`Sys_Milliseconds`' clock) runs 4.2 ppm faster than `mach_absolute_time` (NTP): ~0.02 µs per 4 ms frame.
- **The cores, the session's main finding.** With the new limiter polling the last millisecond (the Vulkan branch's wait), the game's main thread (already `QOS_CLASS_USER_INTERACTIVE`: an app's main thread) ran 84% of its frames on a performance core at 250 fps and 10% at 125; the old limiter 14% and 7%. **On an efficiency core the frame's work takes ~1.4–1.9 ms, on a performance core ~0.16–0.2 ms** (and ~0.4 ms on a performance core at a lower clock). macOS puts a thread that sleeps more than it works on the efficiency cores. The share follows the polled part of the frame (250 fps: 0.3 ms 10%, 1 ms 84%, 2 ms 100%; 125: 1 ms 10%, 2 ms 79%, 4 ms 100%; the whole frame (`com_busyWait 1`) 100%), so the wait polls half of a focused client's frame (100% at 125, 144, 240, 250 and 500 in every run; ~52–55% of a core). Game Mode was on (the Space) in every run; `NSActivityLatencyCritical` didn't help (69% at 250, 11% at 125). The game's process CPU: 250 fps 58% → 59%, 125: 34% → 54%, 500: 57% → 68%, uncapped unchanged (110%).
- **Measured** (§2 "The µs frame limiter", the numbers): exact caps (240 → 239.98, 144 → 143.99; the old limiter ran them at 250.02 and 166.68); every frame starts within ~5 µs of its deadline (old: p99 0.67 ms at 250, 1.5 ms at 125); every frame at 125 / 250 / 500 is 8 / 4 / 2 ms of game time (99.9–100%; the old one gave 7/8/9 at 125, its `select` waking past the tick); commit intervals p99 4.04 ms at 250 (old 4.65), 8.07 at 125 (old 9.66). Frame start → on screen at 250 fps 3.66 → 1.98 ms p50 without input.
- **With mouse input** (the user's OK): continuous movement keeps every variant on the performance cores (pump → present 1.83 ms p50 in all four); from rest, the first frame of a flick: old 1.80–1.85 ms pump → commit (frames 1–5 on the efficiency cores), polling the last millisecond 0.60–0.74, the new 0.25, busy 0.23–0.26 (§2).
- **A dedicated server**: its frames were 0–12 ms late (one long coalesced `select`), now exact (`sv_fps` 20: 50.000 ms every frame, 40: 25.000), for ~2% of a core. A local client connected to it (`build.noindex/s29/svc.sh`, 127.0.0.1): both sides' packets wake the kqueue sleep, the server's frames exact with a client, the client's 4.000 ms. The server's ping column read 0 with the old limiter and 23 with the new: the server measures ping in whole server frames (`svs.time` at the ack minus at the snapshot), and with both limiters exact on one machine's clock the client's packets sometimes arrive just after the next server frame; on a remote server the frames now start on time instead of up to 12 ms late.
- **Queued presents (open, not the limiter's own):** between 18:36 and 18:46 something made the display hold our flips to the 240 Hz refresh for stretches (commit → present jumping to ~10 ms and growing by 0.17 ms per frame, 4.167 − 4.000, until a frame was dropped), only while the synthetic mouse moved in bursts: exact 250 fps then queued 10–21% of the frames (the old limiter 0.1–2.5%, the old limiter with a busy wait, also exact, 9.4%); **240 fps stayed clean (0.0%)** in the middle of it. From 18:48 on, six alternating runs had none with either limiter, and a traced run showed every swap direct at 250/s. Not found in the unified log (no screen capture); the user's Discord was frontmost between runs; asked, the user wasn't sure what was drawing. §6 has the follow-up.
- **Bench** (`build.noindex/s29/ab.sh`, `metalref` = e81733f8, the code of f4f62e27; unlocked; the timedemo's wait polls to the next millisecond as before): two sets of ABBA ×4 (16 runs each): `metal` 0.2156 / 0.2206 ms work (mean of 8), `metalref` 0.2127 / 0.2218 (the second set started with `metalref`); without frame 0, 213.1 / 218.8 against 210.6 / 219.4 µs per frame; pooled p50/p95/p99/p99.9 204/358/472/679 and 209/371/502/970 µs against 203/351/466/646 and 210/372/490/758. **Equal** (~1 µs apart over both sets; the runs spread 203–229 µs).
- **Fixed views** (`port/shot.sh regress base-s9`): identical to the baseline except the views that always vary (dm6 view 8, dm5's snow, dm6fx views 4–7) **and m2l1** (both views, 235–286 pixels, mostly 1–2 levels: the HUD's "Game Saved" fading and the far spot at (753,374)). `metalref` matches base-s9 there (0–1 pixel). The shot cfgs run with the fresh config's `com_maxfps 85`, which the old limiter ran at 90.9 frames a second and the new at 85, and m2l1 has real-time effects: at `com_maxfps 100` the two builds differ by ~7,400 pixels, all in the swaying trees (a crop shows the trees shifted, the rocks and text identical; `cg_rain 0` changes nothing), while each build reproduces itself (0–17 pixels). The trees' sway follows real time somewhere upstream (no code of the renderer changed; `RB_Begin2D` sets the backend's time from `ri.Milliseconds`, one candidate, not chased), and the two limiters reach the screenshot at different real times (no catch-up frame after the load's long frames, exact non-divisor caps). **New baseline `base-s29`** (`port/shot.sh savebase base-s29`, this session's final build): from now on `regress base-s29`; m2l1 reproduces itself within 16–17 pixels at view 0's spot, as before.
- **Checks:** build (game, `omohaaded`; the install signs and checks the seal); `port/glcheck.sh` clean; no new warnings in the changed files.

The clock over 5 minutes: `gettimeofday` +5.4 ppm against `mach_absolute_time` (1.6 ms). The fps counter (`fps 1`) now in µs: 239.9 at 240 on screen (it read 238.8 / 242.4).

- **The proof** (§2 "The decoupled command clock", the table): a local `omohaaded` logging every packet and every usercmd it runs, one client with in-process input (a scratch build, `s30tmp.patch`). At 250 the server gets stock 250 fps's stream in every mode (render 250, 500, 960, uncapped): 250 usercmds and 125 packets a second, lengths 4 / 5 / 2 ms in 92.0 / 5.3 / 2.7% (stock's own time-delta steps), 2 new usercmds per packet, packets 8.000 ms apart (99.4–99.9% within ±125 µs with a cap, 97.8% uncapped, stock 99.4%); at 125 stock 125 fps's (8 / 9 / 6 ms, 1 per packet). At the same pump rate the key values and the down time per press are stock's; rendering faster gives fractions for presses inside a window (the down time kept). The game clock is real time. Every frame on a performance core.
- **Fixed in the session** (the first version against stock, §2): the usercmd's time clamped to `cl.serverTime` (7 ms usercmds at 125 where stock has 6), frames across the usercmd's tick (packets ±300 µs uncapped), the two clocks out of phase at divisor caps (usercmd-only frames at render 250 + usercmds 250).
- **Found: the server aims with the packet's eye angles,** not the usercmd's (`Player` sets `m_vViewAng` from `current_eyeinfo`, `CL_EyeInfo` sends cgame's `refdefViewAngles` once per packet, the last drawn frame's): in stock they trail the packet's newest usercmd by one frame (~4 ms of turning at 250, ~8 at 125); in the decoupled mode by the last drawn frame's age (~0.7 ms at render 960, ~0.1–0.3 ms uncapped). Click-time aim (the second half) must latch them as well as the usercmd's angles.
- **The view:** every drawn frame's view equals the live angle (100% within 0.01°, 31684 frames uncapped, 25077 of them turned without a new usercmd). The movement between usercmds is still the last usercmd's (steps at the usercmd rate while moving): the second half's display.
- **CPU:** uncapped is a full core (no wait); with a cap the wait polls its last millisecond or half the period (§3.1 #19), so caps above ~900 poll the whole wait (a full core, as uncapped), 500 polls half of each 2 ms frame (~60% of a core), render 240 + usercmds 125 half of each 4.2 ms frame. Usercmd-only frames (a usercmd ahead of the cap's frame) cost 13–15 µs p50, 31–48 µs p99 (render 960 and 240), and draw nothing.

- **The 0 ms frame pass** (by reading the code, no run): cgame's effects are time-based (emitters spawn `dtime × detail / spawnRate`, temp models and animations by `cg.time`), so 0 ms frames add nothing (emitters then run at stock 1000 fps's rate, a little denser than at 250: stock's own frame-rate dependence when one spawns per call); `cg.frametime` and `cls.frametime` have no divisions; `wait N` counts game milliseconds (0 ms frames don't count down) and `wait 1` is one frame (shorter in real time at high rates; a bind's release without a key time adds half the usercmd window, as stock's half frame); sound updates by its own clock; AVI recording runs the stock mode (`CL_CmdInterval`).

Session 32 result (the decoupled mode's view latency; the user at the machine at the start (HIDIdleTime 44 s): "Run them now" for the runs that take the cursor, "Yes, run them" for the synthetic-mouse probe, "Run without me"; then away; commits on `metal`, not pushed: Phase 5 isn't done):
- **The slow-frame tail was a bug of the mode** (§2 "The decoupled mode's slow frames, and the render rate by the input's age on screen"): rerun on plain `metal` it stayed (pump → commit p95 0.55–0.56 against 0.25 ms); a scratch frame log by section put it in cgame's prediction, replaying up to 64 usercmds a frame for ~10 s after the loading screen because the in-process server ran none of them. A usercmd made while primed stamped the next one with the client's uptime (the time delta is set at the first snapshot); the first usercmd after the loading screen's pause stepped back 9.5 s, the 8-bit step encoding made that +218 ms, and the server dropped every usercmd below it. Online a client whose uptime is ahead of a fresh server's clock would have stood still for the difference.
- **Fixed** (`cl_input.cpp`, §3.2 "Added in session 32"): the mode starts with the first snapshot (`CA_ACTIVE`); a usercmd's time is never below the one before it. Pump → commit 0.19 / 0.23 / 0.28 ms at render 250, stock's; the server runs every usercmd from the unpause on.
- **The probe's new line, "input age on screen (by time)"** (`cl_latency.cpp`), and `port/latency.sh`'s `MOUSEHZ` (997: the synthetic events drift through every phase of the game's millisecond within a run; at 1000 the phase stays put for a whole run and newest event → pump read 0.44–1.26 ms run to run).
- **The render rate** (§2's table; the Space, 1080p, two runs each, alternating): input age on screen p50 / p95 stock 250 4.62 / 6.55, the mode at 250 4.62 / 6.54, **480 3.59 / 4.76**, 960 3.55 / 4.69, uncapped 3.16 / 4.25 ms. 960 gains nothing over 480 (half its frames never show); 3 drawables: same at 480, ~0.9 ms worse at 960; late presents off at 480: same. Uncapped's last ~0.4 ms is its GPU never idling (pump → GPU done 0.47 against 0.64–0.70 ms). Not kept (scratch prototypes in `s32x`): a render frame that starts when a drawable is ready (3.64, like 480), and keeping the GPU awake during the frame wait (no change anywhere). **Recommended:** `com_maxfps 480` in the mode; uncapped for ~0.4 ms more at a whole core and ~3500 GPU frames a second (§4b "Recommended setup").
- **Not reached:** terrain and ladders in the mode (session 33).
- **Bench** (`port/bench.sh run metal 3`, the stock mode): 0.225 / 0.224 / 0.218 ms work, pooled p50/p95/p99/p99.9 212/380/499/755 µs: equal to session 31. The stock path's only change is `CL_CmdInterval`'s state test (cl_cmdRate 0 returns first) and the probe (off by default). No rendering change, so no fixed views.
- **Checks:** build (game, `omohaaded`; the install signs and checks the seal); `port/glcheck.sh` clean; no new warnings in the changed files.
- **Tools** (scratch, `build.noindex/s32/`): `build.sh` (the flavor `s32x` = HEAD + the work tree's diff + `inst32.py`), `inst32.py` (`s32log 1` + `s32dump <file>`: a per-frame log in memory dumped at quit: the frame's start, the end of each section in µs (IN_Frame, event loop + Cbuf, `SV_Frame`, event loop 2, `CL_SendCmd`, before `SCR_UpdateScreen`, BeginFrame, `CG_DrawActiveFrame` start / end, before / after EndFrame, `CL_Frame`, the frame's end), the cores at start and end, msec, render, the usercmd number, the server's packets / usercmds run / snapshots, the client's snapshots, the newest usercmd's time, `cl.serverTime`, the time delta, `cls.realtime`, the server's last incoming usercmd time, `lastUsercmd.serverTime`, `svs.time`, `paused`; and the two prototypes, `s32pace 1` and `s32wake 1`), `probe32.sh "<name> <args>" …` (probe runs in the Space, waits for 30 s without input; `FLAV=s32x` adds the frame log and `fl32.py`'s summary; `MOUSEHZ` passes through), `fl32.py <log> [slow µs]` (sections p50 / p95, slow against fast frames, the server's counts, the stretches), `sum32.py <probeN.txt>…` (one table line per run), `probe1.txt`…`probe8.txt`, `logs/`.
